162A Orders are confirmable instruments
163 Regulations relating to methodologies and verifiers
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
prescribing the data or other information that must be collected under section 62(1)﻿(a) in respect of an activity, and, if relevant, the mechanism or method by which the data or information must be collected; and
authorising, in respect of a forestry activity, the EPA to specify the location where, and the device by which, the data or other information prescribed in accordance with paragraph (a) must be collected; and
prescribing a methodology or methodologies for calculating emissions or removals from an activity for the purposes of section 62(1)﻿(b); and
prescribing the data or other information, or the calculations of emissions or removals, that must be verified by a person or organisation recognised by the EPA under section 92; and
authorising the EPA to issue guidelines or standards by notice in relation to—
the matters prescribed under paragraph (a); and
the method and format for determining the spatial extent of an area of forest land; and
prescribing, for the purposes of section 92,—
the process by which a person or organisation may be recognised as being able to verify information or calculations for the purposes of section 62(1)﻿(a) or (c) or unique emissions factors for the purposes of regulations made under section 164; and
the expertise, technical competence, or qualifications required for recognition as a person or organisation able to verify unique emissions factors or information relating to 1 or more types of data or information, the calculations of certain types of emissions or removals, or 1 or more activities; and
any additional—
requirements for recognition of an organisation; and
restrictions on the employees of the organisation who may carry out the duties of the organisation in respect of the recognition; and
the period for which a person or organisation may be recognised, and the process for the renewal of recognition; and
conditions of recognition, which may include (but are not limited to) ongoing competency and professional standard requirements, membership of a professional body, and the provision of reports to the EPA; and
the procedure for, and circumstances in which, recognition may be suspended or revoked; and
fees to enable the recovery of the direct and indirect costs of the EPA in recognising a person or organisation, which may vary depending on the class of persons or organisations, or the type of verification in respect of which recognition is sought.
A regulation made under subsection (1) may apply—
generally or with respect to different classes of activity, persons, parts of New Zealand, or other things; or
in respect of the same classes of activity, persons, parts of New Zealand, or other things, in different circumstances; or
generally or at any specified time of each year.
A regulation made under subsection (1)﻿(a) to (d) may have retrospective effect if the regulation is expressed to apply from the commencement of the year in which it is made, or in respect of a period after any particular date within the year in which it is made.
A regulation made under subsection (1)﻿(b), and any associated regulations made under other paragraphs of subsection (1),—
may, without limiting subsection (1), relate to emissions or removals that—
stem directly from an activity; or
are associated with a product or other thing that is the subject of the activity; and
may require the use of a computer programme available via the Internet site of the EPA; and
must not cover any emissions in respect of which another person is required to surrender units or any removals of greenhouse gases in respect of which another person is entitled to a transfer of New Zealand units under this Act.
In recommending the making of regulations under subsection (1)﻿(a) or (b), the Minister must have regard to international climate change obligations (if any) in respect of the collection of data and information relating to, and the measurement of, emissions and removals from the activity.
A person who has complied with guidelines or standards issued by the EPA in regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(d) is, in the absence of proof to the contrary, presumed to have complied with the relevant requirements specified in regulations corresponding to those guidelines or standards.
Regulations under this section—
are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements); and
have effect in accordance with subsection (3) even if they are not yet published.
If regulations authorise the EPA to issue guidelines or standards under subsection (1)﻿(d),—
those guidelines or standards are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements); and
the regulations must contain a statement to that effect.
164 Regulations relating to unique emissions factors
If regulations made under section 163(1)﻿(b) require emissions or removals to be calculated by reference to a default emissions factor, the Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations—
providing for a process by which a participant may apply to the EPA for approval to use a unique emissions factor:
prescribing the information that must be collected to support an application for use of a unique emissions factor:
prescribing the criteria for a unique emissions factor, which may include (but are not limited to)—
the percentage by which a unique emissions factor must vary from the default emissions factor, before an application for a unique emissions factor may be made:
the types of greenhouse gases to be reflected in the unique emissions factor:
how the unique emissions factor is to be calculated:
any criteria by which the default emissions factor has been set, that reflect the matters in section 163(4):
a requirement that the unique emissions factor be verified by a recognised verifier.
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).

section 163 (methodologies and verifiers):
section 164 (unique emissions factors).

167 Regulations relating to fees and charges
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council, make regulations prescribing the amount of any fees payable under the ETS participant provisions and the procedures for payment.
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council, make regulations prescribing the fees or charges payable by a person—
who has made an application for an emissions ruling under section 107, to enable the recovery of all or part of the direct and indirect costs of the EPA in—
receiving and processing the application; and
considering whether to make the ruling, making the ruling, or declining to make the ruling; or
who is a participant, or who has applied to be a participant, in respect of a removal activity, to enable the recovery of all or part of the direct and indirect costs of the EPA in doing 1 or more of the following in relation to the activity:
publicising and informing people about the operation of the ETS participant provisions:
administering the operation of the ETS participant provisions:
enforcing and monitoring compliance with the ETS participant provisions:
doing anything else authorised or required under the ETS participant provisions; or
who enters an application into a ballot for a LUC class 6 land permit; or
who submits an input return under section 194, to enable the recovery of all or part of the direct and indirect costs of the EPA in doing calculations and giving notice under section 194A.
Examples of the costs that may be recovered under subsection (2) include (but are not limited to)—
the cost of processing applications and returns:
the costs of providing, operating, and maintaining systems, databases, and other processes in connection with—
the making of emissions rulings; or
the administration of the ETS participant provisions in relation to a removal activity; or
input returns:
the costs of services provided by third parties.
Regulations made under subsection (2) may—
specify the persons or classes of persons by whom any fees and charges prescribed or fixed are payable; and
provide for partial cost recovery from one class of persons and full cost recovery from another (if this is desirable to further the purposes of this Act); and
prescribe the matters for which direct and indirect costs may be recovered; and
prescribe a scale of fees and charges, or a rate based on the time involved in carrying out the function or duty or in exercising the power; and
prescribe a scale of fees and charges, or a fee or charge for a prescribed function, power, or duty; and
prescribe a formula for fixing fees and charges; and
prescribe an annual fee or charge, or classes of fees or charges, payable by participants or classes of participants; and
prescribe the time of payment of fees and charges, the means of collection of fees and charges, and the person who is responsible for paying a fee or charge; and
authorise the EPA to recover the full costs of services from third parties (other than services in respect of which a fee or charge is prescribed) in circumstances prescribed in the regulations; and
authorise the EPA to grant, in whole or in part, an exemption, waiver, or refund in relation to any fee or charge.
Subsection (2) is subject to sections 173(2) and 174(1) (which relate to material incorporated by reference).
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
If regulations authorise the EPA to grant exemptions, waivers, or refunds referred to in subsection (4)﻿(j),—
an instrument granting an exemption or a waiver or refund is secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements), unless it applies only to 1 or more named persons; and
the regulations must contain a statement to that effect.

167B Regulations relating to LUC class 6 land permits
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:

specifying the time frames within which applications to enter the first ballot or the second ballot may be made each year:
specifying how the total number of hectares in the annual hectare limit must be apportioned between the first and second ballots:
prescribing how much of the annual hectare limit must be reserved for small applications in each of the first and second ballots:
specifying the proportion of the annual hectare limit that comprises the roll-over limit:
prescribing the number of hectares of LUC class 6 land in respect of which an application is made that determines whether an application is to be treated as a small application:
requiring an application for a LUC class 6 land permit to include a map of the applicant’s LUC class 6 land prepared in accordance with any mapping standard issued by the EPA:
requiring a permit application to identify the applicant's LUC class 6 land in respect of which the application is made by reference to—
the NZLRI map; or
a property-scale assessment undertaken in accordance with any mapping standard issued by the EPA:
requiring a person who undertook a property-scale assessment for inclusion in an application for a LUC class 6 land permit to provide information to the EPA about the assessment when requested to do so, for example, a statement that the person undertook the assessment in accordance with any methodology specified in regulations:
prescribing the process the EPA must follow after a ballot is conducted, including—
determining whether an application meets the conditions of eligibility; and
notifying applicants whose applications are successful:
specifying the circumstances in which part of the annual hectare limit of LUC class 6 land in a ballot or the unused hectares of an expired or a cancelled permit may be transferred to or added to a later year’s ballot:
prescribing whether, how, and how many hectares that are not included in the LUC class 6 permits that are issued as the result of a ballot may be reallocated (except as provided in section 190KK(2)﻿(a) or (b) or section 190KL), including in either or both of the following ways:
to another ballot:
to the allocation of hectares that are reserved or not reserved for small applications:
prescribing any other matters that are necessary to ensure the proper functioning of a ballot.
Regulations made under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).

169 Incorporation by reference in certain regulations
The following written material may be incorporated by reference in regulations made under a relevant empowering section:
decisions, computer programmes, rules, guidelines, principles, measures, methodologies, modalities, procedures, mechanisms, or other matters; and
any standards, requirements, or recommended practices of a government agency, standard-setting organisation, or professional body.
Material may be incorporated by reference in regulations—
in whole or in part; and
with modifications, additions, or variations specified in the regulations.
Material incorporated by reference in regulations has legal effect as part of the regulations.
In this section and sections 170 to 174, relevant empowering section means section 161A, 163, 164, 167, 168, 180G, 181W, 190F, 191I, 192U, 193R, 194B, 196F, or 197A.
170 Effect of amendments to, or replacement of, material incorporated by reference in regulations
Subsection (2) applies to an amendment to, or a replacement of, material if the material—
is incorporated by reference in regulations made under a relevant empowering section (the original regulations); and
is adopted, agreed on, made, or approved by an international government agency, international organisation, or international professional body.
The amendment or replacement of the material has legal effect as part of the regulations only if regulations made under the relevant empowering section after the original regulations were made state that the particular amendment or replacement has that effect.
Subsection (4) applies to an amendment to, or a replacement of, material if the material—
is incorporated by reference in regulations made under a relevant empowering section (the original regulations); and
is not material described by subsection (1)﻿(b).
The amendment or replacement of the material has immediate legal effect as part of the original regulations (without the need for an amendment to the original regulations, or the making of other regulations, to state that effect).
171 Proof of material incorporated by reference
A copy of any material incorporated by reference in regulations, including any amendment to, or replacement of, the material (material) must be—
certified as a correct copy of the material by the chief executive; and
retained by the chief executive.
The production in proceedings of a certified copy of the material incorporated by reference is, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, sufficient evidence that the material produced is the material incorporated by reference in regulations.
172 Effect of expiry of material incorporated by reference
This section applies to material incorporated by reference in regulations, made under a relevant empowering section, that expires, is revoked, or ceases to have effect without being amended or replaced.
The material ceases to have legal effect as part of the regulations only if regulations made under the relevant empowering section state that the material ceases to have legal effect.
173 Requirement to consult
This section applies to regulations made under a relevant empowering section that—
incorporate material by reference; or
state, as required by section 170(2), that an amendment to, or a replacement of, material incorporated by reference in regulations has legal effect as part of the regulations.
Before regulations to which this section applies are made, the chief executive must—
make copies of the material proposed to be incorporated by reference, or the proposed amendment to or replacement of material incorporated by reference (proposed material), available for inspection during working hours for a reasonable period, free of charge, at the office of the chief executive; and
make copies of the proposed material available for purchase at a reasonable price; and
give notice in the Gazette stating—
that the proposed material is available for inspection during working hours, free of charge; and
the place where the proposed material can be inspected, and the period during which it can be inspected; and
that copies of the proposed material can be purchased; and
the place where the proposed material can be purchased; and
allow a reasonable opportunity for persons to comment on the proposal to incorporate the proposed material by reference; and
consider any comments these persons make.
The reference in subsection (2) to the proposed material includes, if the material is not in an official New Zealand language, an accurate translation of the material in an official New Zealand language.
Before regulations to which this section applies are made, the chief executive—
may make copies of the proposed material available in any other way that the chief executive considers appropriate in the circumstances (for example, via an Internet site); and
must, if paragraph (a) applies, give notice in the Gazette stating that the proposed material is available in other ways and details of where or how it can be accessed or obtained.
A failure to comply with this section does not invalidate regulations that incorporate material by reference.
174 Public access to material incorporated by reference
The chief executive—
must make the material specified in subsection (2) (material) available for inspection during working hours, free of charge, at the office of the chief executive; and
must make copies of the material available for purchase at a reasonable price at the office of the chief executive; and
may make copies of the material available in any other way that the chief executive considers appropriate in the circumstances (for example, via an Internet site); and
must give notice in the Gazette stating—
that the material is incorporated in the regulations and—
the date on which the regulations were made; or
if the material has immediate legal effect under section 170(4), the date on which it had legal effect; and
that the material is available for inspection during working hours, free of charge; and
the place where it can be inspected; and
that copies of the material can be purchased; and
the place where the material can be purchased; and
that, if copies of the material are made available under paragraph (c), the material is available in other ways and the details of where or how the material can be accessed or obtained.
The material is—
material incorporated by reference in regulations made under a relevant empowering section:
any amendment to, or replacement of, that material that is incorporated in the regulations or the material specified in paragraph (a) with the amendments or replacement material incorporated:
if the material specified in paragraph (a) or (b) is not in an official New Zealand language, an accurate translation of the material in an official New Zealand language.
A failure to comply with this section does not invalidate regulations that incorporate material by reference.
175 Application of Legislation Act 2019 to material incorporated by reference
Subpart 1 of Part 3 and section 114 of the Legislation Act 2019 do not apply to material that is incorporated by reference in regulations merely because it is incorporated.
176 Application of Regulations (Disallowance) Act 1989 to material incorporated by reference
177 Application of Standards and Accreditation Act 2015 not affected
Sections 169 to 176 do not affect the application of sections 29 to 32 of the Standards and Accreditation Act 2015.
178 Recovery of fees or charges
A fee or charge that is not paid in accordance with regulations made under this Part may be recovered from the person liable to pay the fee or charge by the EPA in any court of competent jurisdiction.
The EPA may enter into any agreement or arrangement, on any terms that the EPA thinks fit, with any person to collect, or assist in the collection of, any fees or charges that are payable.
178A Option to pay money instead of surrendering, repaying, or reimbursing units
This section applies if—
either—
a person is required to surrender or repay units—
as a result of submitting an emissions return; or
under section 123(3) or (6) or 180C(2)﻿(b); or
the EPA is required by this Act to reimburse units to any person; and
the units that must be surrendered, repaid, or reimbursed are in respect of,—
for an activity of standard forestry, emissions or removals that are attributed by section 178C(3) to a calendar year that ends before the start date for auctions; or
for any other activity, emissions or removals that occurred in a calendar year that ends before the start date for auctions; or
an activity that the person ceased to participate in, and for which the person has submitted an emissions return, before the start date for auctions.
If subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(i) applies, for the purposes of this section, the units in respect of the emissions or removals attributed to a calendar year must be rounded to the nearest whole number (where 0.5 is rounded up).
Despite anything in this Act, if this section applies, a person may satisfy the person’s obligation to surrender, repay, or reimburse units,—
in the case of a person other than the EPA, by—
surrendering or repaying the units in accordance with the relevant provision; or
paying the fixed price for each unit that the person is liable to surrender or repay, into a Crown Bank Account, by the date or within the period by which the units are required to be surrendered or repaid; or
a combination of the actions provided for in subparagraphs (i) and (ii); or
in the case of the EPA, by—
reimbursing units to a person in accordance with the procedure specified in section 124; or
paying the fixed price for each unit into a bank account designated by the person; or
a combination of the actions provided for in subparagraphs (i) and (ii).
For the purposes of subsection (2)﻿(a)﻿(ii) and (iii), a person’s obligation to surrender units or repay units is only satisfied when the funds paid into a Crown Bank Account are cleared.
For the purposes of subsection (3) and section 178B(1), funds paid into a Crown Bank Account are to be treated as cleared when it is no longer possible to reverse the payment and the funds are available for use by the Crown.
In this section and sections 178B and 178C,—
fixed price has the meaning given by section 178C
start date for auctions means the date specified by regulations made under section 30GA(2)﻿(a) as the date on which the sale of New Zealand units by auction commences.
178B Issuing New Zealand units to meet surrender obligations
If, in accordance with section 178A(2)﻿(a)﻿(ii) or (iii), a person pays the fixed price instead of surrendering a unit that the person is liable to surrender, the Registrar must, when the funds are cleared,—
issue a number of New Zealand units into a Crown holding account equal to the number of units in respect of which the person has paid the fixed price for each unit; and
transfer the New Zealand units into the person’s holding account held for the purpose of section 61(1); and
immediately following the transfer under paragraph (b), transfer the New Zealand units to a surrender account designated by the EPA.
The Registrar may, for the purposes of subsection (1)﻿(a), issue a number of New Zealand units equal to the number of units in respect of which 1 or more persons have paid the fixed price for each unit under section 178A(2)﻿(a)﻿(ii) or (iii).
If the EPA is required by this Act to reimburse units to any person and has satisfied its obligation to do so by paying to the person the fixed price for each unit in accordance with section 178A(2)﻿(b)﻿(ii) or (iii), then the Registrar must—
transfer from the appropriate surrender account to the person’s holding account held for the purpose of section 61(1) a number of New Zealand units equal to the number of units for which the EPA paid the person the fixed price for each unit; and
immediately following the transfer under paragraph (a), transfer the New Zealand units from the person’s holding account to a cancellation account.
For the avoidance of doubt, section 68 does not apply in respect of any New Zealand units issued under this section.
If subsection (1) applies, this Act applies with any necessary modification as if the payment of the fixed price for a unit by a person and the transfer of a unit to a surrender account by the Registrar under this section were a surrender of a unit by the person.
Despite anything in section 18CA(2), a New Zealand unit that is transferred to a surrender account under subsection (1)﻿(c) may be further transferred in accordance with subsection (3)﻿(a).
178C Fixed price (for option to pay money instead of surrendering, repaying, or reimbursing units)
This section determines the fixed price of a unit for the purposes of sections 178A and 178B.
If the unit that must be surrendered, repaid, or reimbursed is in respect of emissions or removals that occurred in a calendar year that is—
2019 or earlier, the fixed price is $25; or
2020 or later, but that ends before the start date for auctions, the fixed price is $35.
For the purposes of section 178A, if an emissions return covers more than 1 calendar year of an activity of standard forestry, emissions and removals are attributed to, and treated as having occurred in, each year in the proportion that the year bears to the emissions return period.
 Part 5 — Sector-specific provisions: forestry
 Subpart 1 — Deforestation
179 Forest land to be treated as deforested in certain cases
Without limiting paragraph (a) of the definition of deforest in section 4(1), a hectare of forest land must be treated as deforested for the purposes of this Act if the forest species on that hectare have been cleared and,—
4 years after clearing, none of the following apply:
the hectare has at least 500 stems of exotic forest species growing:
the hectare has been replanted with at least 100 stems of willows or poplars in a manner consistent with managing soil erosion:
the hectare has predominantly indigenous forest species growing in a manner in which the hectare is likely to be forest land 10 years after the hectare was cleared; or
10 years after clearing,—
predominantly exotic forest species are growing, but that hectare does not have tree crown cover of more than 30% from trees that have reached 5 metres in height; or
predominantly indigenous forest species are growing, but that hectare is not forest land; or
20 years after clearing, predominantly indigenous forest species are growing, but that hectare does not have tree crown cover of more than 30% from trees that have reached 5 metres in height.
Subsection (1)﻿(a)﻿(ii) applies only if the EPA is satisfied that the relevant local authority has determined that the soil erosion risk of the land is at least moderate.
If forest land is to be treated as deforested under subsection (1),—
the deforestation is to be treated as having been carried out 4 years, 10 years, or 20 years after the clearing of the forest species, as the case may be; but
the liability in respect of the deforestation must be calculated by reference to the age and forest species of the trees cleared 4 years, 10 years, or 20 years earlier, as the case may be.
Nothing in this section limits the EPA's ability to exercise powers under section 121 in respect of the deforestation of a hectare of forest land whenever the EPA considers that—
the hectare has been converted to land that is not forest land; and
any obligations imposed under this Act in respect of the deforestation have not been complied with.
179A Forest land may not be treated as deforested in certain cases
Despite section 179 and the definition of deforest in section 4(1),—
in the case of pre-1990 forest land, pre-1990 forest land that is cleared may not be treated as deforested for the purposes of this Act if the cleared land is exempt land or—
is contiguous with the edge of pre-1990 forest land that existed on 31 December 2007; and
is an area that is less than 1 hectare or that is less than 30 metres wide at its widest point; and
is required to be or remain cleared to implement New Zealand's best practice forest management; and
is used only for the purpose of implementing New Zealand's best practice forest management:
in the case of pre-1990 forest land in relation to which a P90 offset application has been submitted under section 181A, the land may not be treated as deforested during the period—
starting on the date on which the application is submitted; and
ending on the P90 offset date for the land under section 181F(5):
in the case of post-1989 forest land, the post-1989 forest land that is cleared may not be treated as deforested if the cleared land—
is contiguous with the edge of post-1989 forest land that existed on the date of the first registration of any person as a participant in standard forestry or permanent forestry in respect of the cleared land; and
is an area that is less than 1 hectare or that is less than 30 metres wide at its widest point; and
is required to be or remain cleared to implement New Zealand's best practice forest management; and
is used only for the purpose of implementing New Zealand's best practice forest management.
However, in relation to subsection (1)﻿(b), see section 181D, under which the effect of subsection (1)﻿(b) may be reversed.
If subsection (1)﻿(c) applies (where land is cleared for forest management), see sections 182G, 186A, and 186C.
This section applies to land that was cleared before, on, or after the commencement of this section.
If regulations prescribe any meaning for New Zealand’s best practice forest management, then that term has (or includes) that meaning in this section.
 Subpart 2 — Pre-1990 forest land
180 Participant in respect of pre-1990 forest land
If the activity listed in Part 1 of Schedule 3 is carried out, the landowner of the pre-1990 forest land is to be treated as the person carrying out the activity unless the EPA is satisfied that—
the right to decide to deforest the pre-1990 forest land was vested by the landowner in a third party, whether before or after 1 January 2008; and
the landowner had no control over the decision.
If the EPA is satisfied that the criteria specified in subsection (1)﻿(a) and (b) are met, the third party is to be treated as the person carrying out the activity.
To avoid doubt, for the purposes of this Act, no person, other than a landowner or, in the circumstances in subsection (2), a third party, is to be treated as carrying out an activity listed in Part 1 of Schedule 3.
180A When deforestation to be treated as occurring in respect of pre-1990 forest land
This section applies to any hectare of pre-1990 forest land that is being converted to land that is not forest land.
The hectare of forest land is to be treated as being deforested on the date of the first action on it that is inconsistent with it remaining forest land.
However, subsection (4) applies if the hectare of forest land was cleared but not deforested prior to—
the forest land being transferred to the landowner; or
control of the forest land reverting to that landowner following the expiry or termination of a forestry right, Crown forestry licence, lease, or other agreement that relates to the land.
The hectare of forest land is to be treated as being deforested on the date of the first action on it that—
is inconsistent with the hectare remaining forest land; and
happens after the date of transfer of the land or the date of the expiry or termination of the forestry right, Crown forestry licence, lease, or other agreement relating to the land.
In any case, the liability in respect of the deforestation must be calculated by reference to the age and forest species of the trees when they were cleared, unless section 180H(2) applies.
This section applies only if section 4(5) does not apply.
To avoid doubt, this section does not apply—
to land to which section 179A applies (and which therefore may not be treated as deforested); or
to land to which section 181N(3) applies.
180B Applications for exemption for land holdings of less than 50 hectares of pre-1990 forest land
This section applies to a person who—
is a landowner of an area of pre-1990 forest land at the date of issue of the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan; or
was the landowner of an area of pre-1990 forest land at the date (if any), between 1 January 2008 and the date of issue of the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan, on which the area was converted to land that is not forest land.
A person to whom this section applies may apply to the EPA for the area of pre-1990 forest land to be declared exempt land if—
the area is less than 50 hectares; and
the area was owned on 1 September 2007 by a person or persons who, along with any associated persons, owned in total less than 50 hectares of pre-1990 forest land; and
no allocation of units to a landowner has been made in respect of the area under the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan.
An application under subsection (2) must—
be submitted to the EPA by—
the date prescribed by regulations made under section 168(1)﻿(ca); or
in the absence of a date prescribed by regulations made under section 168(1)﻿(ca), the date specified by public notice given by the EPA; and
be in the prescribed form and accompanied by the prescribed fee (if any); and
contain details of the area of pre-1990 forest land to which the application relates; and
be accompanied by evidence showing that the land is pre-1990 forest land; and
be accompanied by a statutory declaration,—
in the case of land owned by a sole professional trustee or owned by professional trustees only, from the trustee of the trust that is the subject of the exemption application stating that the total of pre-1990 forest land held in the trust on 1 September 2007—
was less than 50 hectares; and
was owned by a sole professional trustee or owned by professional trustees only:
in any other case, from each person who owned the land on 1 September 2007 (other than a joint tenant who is a professional trustee) stating that the person, together with any persons associated with that person, owned less than a total of 50 hectares of pre-1990 forest land on 1 September 2007; and
be signed by the applicant; and
be accompanied by any other prescribed information.
If the EPA is satisfied that the applicant is a person to whom this section applies, the land is pre-1990 forest land, and each of the criteria specified in subsection (2)﻿(a) to (c) is met, the EPA must—
declare the land to be exempt land; and
notify the applicant that the land has been declared exempt land.
Despite subsection (3)﻿(a), the EPA may, at its discretion, accept applications after the date specified in the public notice given under subsection (3)﻿(a)﻿(ii) or prescribed by regulations under section 168(1)﻿(ca).
The following rules apply for the purposes of determining, under subsection (2)﻿(b), whether an area of pre-1990 forest land was owned on 1 September 2007 by a person or persons who, along with any associated persons, owned in total less than 50 hectares of pre-1990 forest land:
the EPA must consider only pre-1990 forest land in respect of which the person or associated person was a landowner on 1 September 2007; and
if land was owned by persons as joint tenants,—
in the case where 1 or more of the joint tenants is a professional trustee, each of the joint tenants other than the professional trustee or trustees must individually have been a landowner of less than 50 hectares of pre-1990 forest land; or
in the case where none of the joint tenants is a professional trustee, each of the joint tenants must individually have been a landowner of less than 50 hectares of pre-1990 forest land; and
if land was owned by persons as tenants in common, each tenant in common’s interest in the land is to be treated as a divided interest on 1 September 2007; and
if land was owned by a sole professional trustee or owned by professional trustees only, the total pre-1990 forest land held in the trust on 1 September 2007 was less than 50 hectares.
For the purposes of this section and section 180D,—
own, in relation to pre-1990 forest land, means to be a landowner of the land
professional trustee—
means a trustee whose profession, employment, or business is or includes acting as a trustee or investing money on behalf of others; and
includes a trustee in whom property is vested under Te Ture Whenua Maori Act 1993.
180C Certain applications not otherwise permitted by section 180B
Despite section 180B(2)﻿(c) and (3)﻿(a), a person may make an application under section 180B by 31 December 2013 if—
the area concerned was owned, as at 1 September 2007, by a sole professional trustee or by professional trustees only; and
an allocation of units has been made before the commencement of the Climate Change Response (Emissions Trading and Other Matters) Amendment Act 2012 in respect of the area under the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan.
If the EPA proposes to accept the application, the EPA must notify the applicant that—
it proposes to accept the application; but
the applicant must first, within 30 working days after receiving the notice, surrender or repay to the Crown holding account specified in the notice the number of units specified in the notice; and
if the units are not surrendered or repaid in accordance with paragraph (b), then the application will be declined.
The units referred to in subsection (2) must be the same number of units that have been allocated and transferred under the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan in relation to the land concerned.
The EPA must—
accept the application and declare the area concerned to be exempt land if, by the expiry of the 30 days, the units have been surrendered or repaid; or
decline the application if, by the expiry of the 30 days, the units have not been surrendered or repaid.
To avoid doubt,—
section 180B otherwise applies to an application permitted by this section, but subject to the modifications made by this section; and
if an application is granted and an area is declared to be exempt land, the entitlement to units under the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan in respect of the land is cancelled.
180D Applications for exemption for some Maori land or land with 10 or more owners
This section applies to an area of pre-1990 forest land that—
is less than 50 hectares; and
on 1 September 2007,—
was all of the pre-1990 forest land held in a document that is equivalent to a record of title under the Land Transfer Act 2017 or, if there was no such document, in another instrument of title; and
was Maori land or was owned by more than 10 persons; and
was an area of pre-1990 forest land on the following date (the qualifying date):
the date of issue of the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan; or
the date (if any), between 1 January 2008 and the date of issue of the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan, on which the area was converted to land that is not forest land; and
after the qualifying date,—
became owned by the trustees of a trust; or
in the case of Maori freehold land, had an agent appointed for it under Te Ture Whenua Maori Act 1993 with the power to apply under this section; and
has not been the subject of an allocation of units to a landowner under the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan.
The trustees or agent described in subsection (1)﻿(d) may apply to the EPA for the area of pre-1990 forest land to be declared exempt land.
The application—
may be submitted to the EPA at any time; and
must be in the prescribed form and accompanied by the prescribed fee (if any); and
must contain details of the area of pre-1990 forest land to which the application relates; and
must be accompanied by evidence showing that the land is pre-1990 forest land; and
must be accompanied by a statutory declaration from the applicant stating that the area of pre-1990 forest land was, on 1 September 2007, all of the pre-1990 forest land held in a document that is equivalent to a record of title under the Land Transfer Act 2017 or, if there was no such document, in another instrument of title; and
must be signed by the applicant; and
must be accompanied by any other prescribed information.
If the EPA is satisfied that the applicant is 1 or more trustees or an agent described in subsection (1)﻿(d), that the land is pre-1990 forest land, and that each of the criteria specified in subsection (1)﻿(a) to (e) is met, the EPA must—
declare the land to be exempt land; and
notify the applicant that the land has been declared exempt land.
180E Exemptions for deforestation of land with tree weeds
An application may be made under this section for pre-1990 forest land to be declared exempt land (in relation to deforestation) if a prescribed type of tree weed—
is growing on the land; or
was cleared from the land as part of the deforestation process on or after 1 January 2008.
The application may be made by—
the landowner of the pre-1990 forest land; or
a third party to whom section 180 applies.
The EPA must consider the application against the prescribed criteria and priorities and—
may declare the land, or any part of the land, to be exempt land, if satisfied that—
the applicant is eligible to apply for the exemption under subsection (2); and
the land is pre-1990 forest land; and
the criteria specified in subsection (1) are met; and
must, if the EPA declares any land to be exempt land, notify the applicant of—
the declaration; and
any requirements or conditions that the EPA has decided to impose on the person whose land is exempted.
The EPA—
may declare that a person’s land ceases to be exempt land (under this section) if the person breaches any requirement or condition that the EPA imposed on them for the exempt land; and
if it does so, must notify the person of the declaration.
If a person is convicted of an offence under section 132 or 133 in relation to an application under this section,—
the person must be treated as a person who has failed to submit an annual emissions return in respect of an activity listed in Part 1 of Schedule 3 when required to do so under this Act; and
the EPA must make an assessment of the matters that should have been in the person’s annual emissions return and the number of units the person would have been liable to surrender if the land had not been exempt land; and
the person is liable to surrender the number of units in the assessment under paragraph (b); and
section 123(1) to (3) and the other provisions of this Act apply as if the assessment under paragraph (b) were an assessment under section 121.
180F Effect of exemption
The status of pre-1990 forest land as exempt land runs with the land and is not affected by any change in the ownership of the land.
180G Regulations about exemptions for deforestation of land with tree weeds
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes in relation to exemptions under section 180E:
prescribing the types of tree weed for which an application may be made for pre-1990 forest land to be declared exempt land:
defining those types of tree weed based on any matter, such as the following:
the species of tree weed:
the geographical location of the tree weed:
whether a weed control programme applies to the tree weed:
prescribing processes for making applications to the EPA for an exemption, including fees for applications:
prescribing the information required in an application, including—
information to properly describe or define the land:
evidence about the land and the forest species on the land:
prescribing the criteria and priorities that the EPA must consider in deciding whether to grant an exemption:
prescribing any requirements or conditions that the EPA may impose on a person whose land is exempted, including for weed control on the land:
specifying that 1 or more of the following is different for different types of tree weed:
the process for making the application:
the information required in the application:
the criteria and priorities that the EPA must consider:
any requirements or conditions that the EPA may impose on a person whose land is exempted:
providing for any other matters contemplated by sections 180E and 180F, necessary for their administration, or necessary for giving them full effect.
See sections 3A and 3B for consultation requirements that apply to the making of the regulations.
The regulations come into force 3 months after the date of their publication under the Legislation Act 2019, or on any later date specified in the regulations.
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
180H Methodology for pre-1990 forest land cleared in 8 years or less
Subsection (2) applies where the trees cleared from pre-1990 forest land by a person carrying out the activity in Part 1 of Schedule 3 are 8 years or younger.
If this subsection applies, the participant must,—
for the purposes of sections 62(1)﻿(b) and 65(2)﻿(b), apply any prescribed methodology and calculate and record the emissions from the activity as if the trees cleared from the pre-1990 forest land were trees of the age and species of the oldest trees of the predominant species (as determined by regulations made under section 163) cleared from the pre-1990 forest land during the previous 9 years (excluding any period in which the pre-1990 forest land is temporarily unstocked); and
surrender units under this Act based on emissions calculated and recorded in accordance with paragraph (a).
A methodology for calculating emissions from the activity in Part 1 of Schedule 3 prescribed in regulations under section 163 must relate to the trees that are cleared from the pre-1990 forest land as part of the deforestation activity.
 Subpart 3 — P90 offsetting land
181 Interpretation
In this subpart,—
area 1 (approved) land means land that—
has become area 1 (approved) land under section 181C(2)﻿(a); and
has not ceased to be area 1 (approved) land under section 181N(2)﻿(a) to (d)
area 1 (damaged) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(a)﻿(iii)
area 1 (forested) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(a)﻿(ii)
area 1 (not offset) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(a)﻿(iv)
area 1 (offset) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(a)﻿(i)
area 2 (approved) land means land that—
has become area 2 (approved) land under section 181C(2)﻿(c) or 181J(2)﻿(b); and
has not ceased to be area 2 (approved) land under section 181N(2)﻿(e) to (i)
area 2 (damaged) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(b)﻿(iii)
area 2 (excess) land means land that—
has become area 2 (excess) land under section 181G(3)﻿(b)﻿(iv); and
has not ceased to be area 2 (excess) land under section 181N(5)
area 2 (forested) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(b)﻿(i)
area 2 (non-ETS) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(b)﻿(v)
area 2 (unforested) land has the meaning given in section 181G(3)﻿(b)﻿(ii)
baseline carbon stock has the meaning given in section 181F(2)
baseline date has the meaning given in section 181F(3)
default P90 offset date has the meaning given in section 181F(5)﻿(a)
expected carbon stock has the meaning given in section 181F(4)
P90 offset application means an application submitted to the EPA under section 181A
P90 offset application date means the date on which a P90 offset application is submitted to the EPA under section 181A
P90 offset date has the meaning given in section 181F(5)
P90 offset release criteria has the meaning given in section 181F(1)
P90 offsetting land means land that—
has become P90 offsetting land under section 181N(2)﻿(e); and
has not ceased to be P90 offsetting land under—
section 181R(1)﻿(a), for clearing before the required equivalence date; or
section 181S(1)﻿(a), for deforestation before the required equivalence date; or
section 181T(1)﻿(a), on the required equivalence date
P90 release criteria notice means a notice given under section 181G(1)
required equivalence date has the meaning given in section 181F(6).
 P90 offset application
181A Application to offset land for pre-1990 forest land
The owner of pre-1990 forest land may apply to the EPA to offset other land for the pre-1990 forest land.
The application must—
specify the pre-1990 forest land to which the application relates (area 1); and
specify the land proposed as offsetting land for that pre-1990 forest land (area 2); and
include any information prescribed in regulations made under section 181W.
If area 1 and area 2 are owned by different persons, the application must be made jointly by all the owners of both areas.
The application must—
be signed by all of the applicants; and
be submitted—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
181B Criteria for P90 offset application
If a person submits a P90 offset application, the EPA,—
if satisfied that the criteria in subsection (2) were met on the P90 offset application date, must approve the application; or
otherwise, may decline the application (then see section 181D).
The criteria are that—
the land in area 1 is pre-1990 forest land that is one or other of the following:
land that was first planted before 1 January 1990; or
land that was harvested and re-established after 1 January 1960; and
the land in area 2 is 1 or more of the following:
land that is not forest land on the P90 offset application date but, if it were to become forest land,—
would be post-1989 forest land; and
if it were in a carbon accounting area, would meet the criteria in section 191C for having a first rotation forest:
post-1989 forest land that—
became post-1989 forest land less than 2 years before the P90 offset application date; and
meets the criteria in section 191C for having a first rotation forest (or would do so if it were in a carbon accounting area):
area 2 (excess) land, unless the re-use period prescribed in regulations made under section 181W has expired:
P89 offsetting (excess) land (as defined in section 192), unless the re-use period prescribed in regulations made under section 181W has expired; and
if any of the land in area 2 is in a carbon accounting area, all of the land in the carbon accounting area—
is land to which paragraph (b) applies; and
is part of area 2; and
the total area (whether contiguous or not) of area 2 is equal to or greater than the total area (whether contiguous or not) of area 1; and
each individual parcel that makes up area 2 has an area of at least 1 hectare with an average width of at least 30 metres; and
each person who will be a participant in respect of land in area 2 would be eligible to be a participant under section 57 (but need not comply with the restriction under section 182AB) if the person were carrying out an activity listed in Part 1 of Schedule 4 on that land; and
the EPA is satisfied that, if all of the land in area 1 becomes area 1 (offset) land, on the P90 offset date the P90 offset release criteria are likely to be met; and
any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 181W are met.
181C Effect of approval of P90 offset application
This section applies if the EPA approves a P90 offset application.
Starting on the P90 offset application date,—
area 1 is area 1 (approved) land; and
if any of the land in area 2 is in a carbon accounting area, the participant for that land—
is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the unit balance of that carbon accounting area; and
ceases to be a participant in the relevant activity on that carbon accounting area; and
area 2 is the area 2 (approved) land for area 1.
If subsection (2)﻿(b) applies, the EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
If subsection (2)﻿(b)﻿(i) applies,—
the notice requiring those units to be surrendered (referred to in section 63(4)) must also set out the effect of paragraph (b); and
if the units are not surrendered by the due date, the EPA may revoke the approval by giving written notice to the participant.
If an approval is revoked under subsection (4), this Act applies as if the application had never been made.
However, despite subsection (5), the effect of subsection (2)﻿(b)﻿(ii) is not reversed (so the person is not reinstated as a participant for the carbon accounting area).
181D Effect of P90 offset application being declined
This section applies if—
the EPA declines a P90 offset application; and
an activity listed in Part 1 of Schedule 3 was carried out on any of the land in area 1 (the affected land) on a date (date D) that was on or after the P90 offset application date but before the application was declined.
The owner of the affected land on date D—
is liable to surrender the number of units they would have been required to surrender when that activity was carried out if the application had not been made; and
must comply with sections 56 and 65 in relation to that liability.
The emissions return required under subsection (2)﻿(b) must cover the period starting on the P90 offset application date and ending on date D, as if that period were all part of the immediately preceding year.
 Liability to surrender allocation
181E Allocations for pre-1990 forest land to be surrendered when application approved
This section applies in relation to pre-1990 forest land if—
the land becomes area 1 (approved) land; and
an allocation was made in respect of the land as part of the second tranche (whether or not the units were actually transferred when allocated).
The owner of the land must,—
if they do not have a holding account under section 18A, open a holding account; and
surrender or repay the number of units equal to the number allocated as part of the second tranche by transferring them to a Crown holding account.
The owner must do so within 30 working days after the EPA gives them a notice requiring them to do so.
The notice must specify—
the number of units to be surrendered or repaid; and
the Crown holding account to which the units must be transferred.
In this section, second tranche means the allocation of units under the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan that were transferred on or after 1 January 2013.
 Offsetting on P90 offset date
181F P90 offset release criteria
The P90 offset release criteria in respect of area 1 (approved) land and its area 2 (approved) land are that, on the P90 offset date,—
the area of the area 2 (forested) land and any area 2 (damaged) land is equal to or greater than the area of the area 1 (offset) land; and
the expected carbon stock of the area 2 (forested) land and any area 2 (damaged) land is equal to or greater than the baseline carbon stock for the area 1 (offset) land; and
any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 181W are met.
The baseline carbon stock for area 1 (offset) land is the carbon stock that the land had on the baseline date, determined in accordance with regulations made under section 181W.
The baseline date, for area 1 (approved) land, is—
if, on the P90 offset application date, every hectare of land in area 1 had forest species on it that had tree crown cover of more than 30%, the P90 offset application date; or
if not, the date on which the clearing of area 1 started most recently before the P90 offset application date.
The expected carbon stock,—
of area 2 (forested) land, is the carbon stock that the land is expected to have achieved on the required equivalence date determined in accordance with regulations made under section 181W; and
of area 2 (damaged) land, is the carbon stock that the land would have been expected to have achieved on the required equivalence date had it not been affected by the adverse natural event determined in accordance with regulations made under section 181W.
The P90 offset date, in relation to area 1 (approved) land, is—
the date 4 years after the baseline date (the default P90 offset date); or
if a P90 release criteria notice is given before the default P90 offset date, the date on which the notice is given.
The required equivalence date is the last day of the period that—
starts when the forest species on area 2 (approved) land are first established; and
ends at the end of the usual rotation period prescribed in regulations made under section 181W for the forest species that were on the area 1 (approved) land on the baseline date.
181G P90 release criteria notice
The owners of the land in area 1 and area 2 must give notice (P90 release criteria notice) to the EPA under this section identifying the status of all land in area 1 and area 2 (including any additional land in relation to which an application is being made under section 181H) on the P90 offset date.
The P90 release criteria notice—
may be given before the default P90 offset date (under section 181F(5)﻿(a)); but
if not given before that date, must be given within 60 working days after the default P90 offset date.
The P90 release criteria notice must—
identify all of the land in area 1 as one of the following:
area 1 (offset) land, being all the land in area 1 (other than any area 1 (damaged) land) that, on the P90 offset date,—
has ceased to be forest land; or
has been cleared with the intention that it will cease to be forest land:
area 1 (forested) land, being all the land in area 1 that is still forest land on the P90 offset date, other than any land that is area 1 (offset) land under subparagraph (i)﻿(B):
area 1 (damaged) land, being any land in area 1 that has been affected by a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest on the land:
area 1 (not offset) land, being any land in area 1 that section 181L requires to be identified as area 1 (not offset) land; and
identify all of the land in area 2 as one of the following:
area 2 (forested) land, being all the land in area 2 that is qualifying forest land on the P90 offset date, other than any land that is area 2 (excess) land or area 2 (non-ETS) land:
area 2 (unforested) land, being all the land in area 2 that is not qualifying forest land on the P90 offset date, other than land that is area 2 (damaged) land:
area 2 (damaged) land, being any land in area 2 that, before the P90 offset date, was affected by a natural event that permanently prevents establishing a forest on the land:
area 2 (excess) land, being any land in area 2 that—
is qualifying forest land on the P90 offset date (but see subsection (4)); and
does not need to be included in the area 2 (forested) land in order for the P90 offset release criteria to be met; and
the owners want to be excluded from the area 2 (forested) land and to be available for re-use under section 181B(2)﻿(b)﻿(iii) or 192B(2)﻿(c)﻿(iii):
area 2 (non-ETS) land, being any land in area 2 that—
is qualifying forest land on the P90 offset date (but see subsection (4)); and
does not need to be included in the area 2 (forested) land in order for the P90 offset release criteria to be met; and
the owners want to be excluded from the area 2 (forested) land but not to be available for re-use under section 181B(2)﻿(b)﻿(iii) or 192B(2)﻿(c)﻿(iii); and
specify—
the baseline carbon stock for the area 1 (offset) land; and
the expected carbon stock of the area 2 (forested) land; and
include any information prescribed in regulations made under section 181W.
If an application is made under section 181H to add additional land, the additional land must be identified as area 2 (forested) land.
The P90 release criteria notice must—
be made jointly by all of the persons who, on the P90 offset date, own land in area 1 or area 2 (including any additional land in relation to which an application is made under section 181H); and
be signed by all of the owners; and
be given within 60 working days after the P90 offset date; and
be given—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
Land is qualifying forest land if—
each hectare of the land has forest species on it that have, or are likely to have, tree crown cover of more than 30%; and
those forest species were established by direct planting activities, including direct seeding but excluding natural forest regeneration; and
each individual parcel that makes up the land has an area of at least 1 hectare and has an average width of at least 30 metres.
181H Application to add area 2 (approved) land
An application to add more land (additional land) to the area 2 (approved) land may be made when the P90 release criteria notice is given to the EPA.
The application must—
be made jointly by all of the persons who own land in area 1, area 2, or the additional land; and
be signed by all of the applicants; and
be given—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
181I Criteria for adding area 2 (approved) land
If an application is made under section 181H, the EPA,—
if satisfied that the criteria in subsection (2) are met, must approve the application; or
otherwise, may decline the application.
The criteria are that—
the additional land—
is land of a kind specified in section 181B(2)﻿(b)﻿(ii), (iii), or (iv); and
is qualifying forest land (as defined in section 181G(6)); and
if the application is not approved, on the P90 offset date the P90 offset release criteria in section 181F(1)﻿(a) or (b) (or both) will not be met; and
the area of the additional land is not more than is necessary to enable the P90 offset release criteria to be met; and
any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 181W are met.
181J Effect of approval of application to add area 2 (approved) land
This section applies if the EPA approves an application under section 181H to add land to the area 2 (approved) land.
On the P90 offset date (but before section 181N takes effect),—
if any of the additional land is a carbon accounting area, the participant for that land—
is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the unit balance of that carbon accounting area; and
ceases to be a participant in the relevant activity on that carbon accounting area; and
the additional land becomes part of the area 2 (approved) land (and is therefore part of area 2 and becomes area 2 (forested) land (see section 181G(3)﻿(b)﻿(i) and (4)).
If subsection (2)﻿(a) applies, the EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
If subsection (2)﻿(a)﻿(i) applies,—
the notice requiring those units to be surrendered (referred to in section 63(4)) must also set out the effect of paragraph (b); and
if the units are not surrendered by the due date, the EPA may revoke the approval by giving written notice to the participant.
If an approval is revoked under subsection (4), this Act applies as if the application had never been made, except that—
the effect of subsection (2)﻿(a)﻿(ii) is not reversed (so the person is not reinstated as a participant for the carbon accounting area); and
section 181M does not apply.
181K Effect of application to add area 2 (approved) land being declined
This section applies if the EPA declines an application under section 181H to add land to the area 2 (approved) land.
The EPA must notify the applicants of—
the decision and the reasons for it; and
the owners’ right under section 144 to seek a review of that decision; and
the effect of subsection (3) and section 128A.
If the EPA gives a notice under subsection (2),—
the P90 release criteria notice that was accompanied by the application is taken not to have been given; and
the owners must give the EPA—
a revised P90 release criteria notice for area 1 and area 2; and
if they wish to do so, another application under section 181H to add additional land; and
the deadline for giving the P90 release criteria notice under section 181G(2)﻿(b) is extended to 60 working days after the notice was given under subsection (2).
However, if the owners have previously given a revised notice under subsection (3)﻿(b)﻿(i) that was accompanied by an application under section 181H,—
subsection (3)﻿(b)﻿(ii) does not apply and the owners cannot make another application under section 181H; and
section 181M does not apply.
181L Land that must be identified as area 1 (not offset) land
Land in area 1 must be identified as area 1 (not offset) land if, in the absence of this section,—
the land would be identified as area 1 (offset) land; and
either or both of the following would apply:
the area of the area 1 (offset) land would be greater than the area of the area 2 (forested) land and any area 2 (damaged) land:
the baseline carbon stock for the area 1 (offset) land would be greater than the expected carbon stock of the area 2 (forested) land and any area 2 (damaged) land.
The area of any land identified as area 1 (not offset) land must be the minimum area necessary to result in the P90 offset release criteria being met.
181M Extension of time to add area 2 (approved) land
This section applies if—
a P90 release criteria notice is given to the EPA within the time required by section 181G(2); and
an application under section 181H was not made when the P90 release criteria notice was given; and
the P90 release criteria notice is not a revised notice given under section 181K(3)﻿(b); and
the EPA is not satisfied that the P90 release criteria notice identifies the land in a way that results in the P90 offset release criteria being met.
The EPA may, by giving a notice to the owners of area 1 and area 2, offer them an opportunity to submit a revised P90 release criteria notice with an application under section 181H.
The EPA’s notice must set out—
the reasons for the EPA’s decision that the P90 release criteria notice is not correct; and
the owners’ right under section 144 to seek a review of that decision; and
the effect of subsection (4) and section 128A.
If the EPA gives a notice under subsection (2),—
the P90 release criteria notice referred to in subsection (1)﻿(a) is taken not to have been given; and
the owners must give the EPA—
a revised P90 release criteria notice for area 1 and area 2; and
if they wish to do so, an application under section 181H to add additional land; and
the deadline for giving the P90 release criteria notice under section 181G(2)﻿(b) is extended to 60 working days after the notice was given under subsection (2).
181N Effect on P90 offset date
This section applies if a P90 release criteria notice is given to the EPA in accordance with section 181G.
With effect on the P90 offset date,—
the area 1 (offset) land—
ceases to be area 1 (approved) land; and
is the land against which the area 2 (forested) land and area 2 (damaged) land are compared to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
the area 1 (forested) land—
ceases to be area 1 (approved) land; and
is not part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
the area 1 (damaged) land—
ceases to be area 1 (approved) land; and
is not part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
the area 1 (not offset) land—
ceases to be area 1 (approved) land; and
is not part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
the area 2 (forested) land—
is part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
becomes P90 offsetting land (then see sections 181P to 181T and Part 1A of Schedule 3); and
ceases to be area 2 (approved) land; and
the area 2 (unforested) land—
is not part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
ceases to be area 2 (approved) land; and
the area 2 (damaged) land—
is part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
ceases to be area 2 (approved) land; and
the area 2 (excess) land—
is not part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
ceases to be area 2 (approved) land, but remains area 2 (excess) land subject to subsection (5); and
the area 2 (non-ETS) land—
is not part of the land used to determine whether the P90 offset release criteria are met; and
ceases to be area 2 (approved) land and area 2 (non-ETS) land.
Starting on the P90 offset date, the owner of the area 1 (not offset) land on the P90 offset date—
is liable to surrender units as a result of the deforestation of the land as if that deforestation had occurred on the P90 offset date (see also section 180A(7)﻿(b)); and
must comply with sections 56 and 65 in relation to that liability.
To avoid doubt,—
the owner of the area 1 (offset) land incurs no liability under section 63 for the deforestation (because of section 179A(1)﻿(b)); and
the owner of the area 1 (forested) land is not liable to surrender any units (because the land has not been deforested); and
the owner of the area 1 (damaged) land is not liable to surrender any units (because the land is excluded from Part 1 of Schedule 3).
Land ceases to be area 2 (excess) land if the land—
becomes area 2 (approved) land as a result of being included in a P90 offset application under section 181B(2)﻿(b)﻿(iii); or
becomes P89 offsetting (approved) land as a result of being included in a P89 offset application under section 192B(2)﻿(c)﻿(iii); or
becomes part of a carbon accounting area as a result of an application under section 182C(1) or (3).
The EPA must update the register kept under section 181U to record the effect of this section.
181O Reimbursement of surrendered allocation
This section applies if—
a person surrendered units in respect of area 1 (approved) land in accordance with section 181E(2); and
on the P90 offset date, any of that land is area 1 (forested) land or area 1 (damaged) land.
Starting on the P90 offset date, the EPA is liable to reimburse to that person (whether or not they still own the land) the number of units surrendered under section 181E(2) in respect of that area 1 (forested) land or area 1 (damaged) land.
 P90 offsetting land
181P Participant in respect of P90 offsetting land
If an activity listed in Part 1A of Schedule 3 is carried out on P90 offsetting land, the landowner is to be treated as the person carrying out the activity.
181Q Clearing or deforestation before required equivalence date
This section applies if—
a P90 offset application was approved; and
on the P90 offset date, the area 2 (approved) land became P90 offsetting land for the area 1 (offset) land; and
before the required equivalence date, either or both of the following occur on some or all of the P90 offsetting land (the affected land):
the land is cleared:
an activity listed in Part 1A of Schedule 3 (a Part 1A activity) is carried out on the land.
If the affected land is cleared, section 181R applies in relation to the clearing of the land.
If a Part 1A activity is carried out on the affected land, section 181S applies in relation to that activity.
If the affected land is cleared and later (but still before the required equivalence date) a Part 1A activity is carried out on the affected land,—
if, before the Part 1A activity is carried out, the owner submits an emissions return as required by section 181R(1)﻿(b)﻿(ii),—
section 181R applies in relation to the clearing of the land; and
section 181S does not apply in relation to the Part 1A activity; or
if the owner does not submit an emissions return as required by section 181R(1)﻿(b)﻿(ii) before the Part 1A activity is carried out,—
section 181R does not apply in relation to the clearing of the land; and
section 181S applies in relation to the Part 1A activity.
For the purposes of this section and section 181R, a hectare of P90 offsetting land that has been cleared is to be treated as having been cleared on the first date on which any part of that hectare was cleared.
181R Clearing before required equivalence date
If this section applies in relation to the clearing of affected land, starting on the date on which the clearing occurred,—
the affected land ceases to be P90 offsetting land; and
the owner of the affected land—
is liable to surrender the units as if the affected land were deforested on the date on which it was cleared; and
must submit an emissions return as required by section 65 in relation to that deemed deforestation; and
if they do not have a holding account under section 18A, must open a holding account.
However, the number of units the owner is liable to surrender under subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(i) is the number calculated under subsection (3) instead of the number of units they would otherwise be liable to surrender under section 63.
The number of units to be surrendered (s) is calculated as follows:
where—
is the area of the affected land (in hectares)
is the total area of all of the area 1 (approved) land referred to in section 181Q(1)﻿(b) (in hectares)
is the baseline carbon stock of the area 1 (offset) land, as recorded under section 181U(2)﻿(d) (in tonnes).
In the emissions return required under subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(ii), the relevant portion of the emissions from the area 1 (offset) land is to be recorded as if it were the emissions from deforestation of the affected land.
The liability under subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(i) is to be treated as a liability to surrender units for emissions from the deemed deforestation on the P90 offsetting land.
The EPA must update the register under section 181U to record the effect of this section.
To avoid doubt, the affected land is not to be treated as having been deforested other than as required by this section.
181S Deforestation before required equivalence date
If this section applies in relation to the carrying out of a Part 1A activity on affected land, starting on the date on which the activity was carried out,—
the affected land ceases to be P90 offsetting land; and
the owner of the affected land—
must submit an emissions return as required by section 65 as a result of the carrying out of the activity; but
is liable to surrender the number of units calculated under subsection (2) instead of the number of units they would otherwise be liable to surrender under section 63 for the emissions from the carrying out of the activity.
The number of units to be surrendered (s) is calculated as follows:
where—
is the area of the affected land (in hectares)
is the total area of all of the area 1 (approved) land referred to in section 181Q(1)﻿(b) (in hectares)
is the baseline carbon stock of the area 1 (offset) land, as recorded under section 181U(2)﻿(d) (in tonnes).
In the emissions return required under subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(i), the relevant portion of the emissions from the area 1 (offset) land is to be recorded as if it were the emissions from the carrying out of the activity.
The liability under subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(ii) is to be treated as a liability to surrender units for emissions from the carrying out of the activity on the P90 offsetting land.
The EPA must update the register under section 181U to record the effect of this section.
181T P90 offsetting land becomes pre-1990 forest land
On the required equivalence date, the P90 offsetting land—
ceases to be P90 offsetting land; and
becomes pre-1990 forest land.
The EPA must update the register under section 181U to record the effect of this section.
 Administrative matters
181U P90 offsetting land register
The EPA must keep a register of the following:
P90 offsetting land:
land that has become pre-1990 forest land under section 181T and has not been recorded in the register under section 56:
area 2 (excess) land.
The register must include the following information about the registered land:
whether it is P90 offsetting land, pre-1990 forest land, or area 2 (excess) land:
a description of the land:
the area of the land (in hectares):
for P90 offsetting land, the baseline carbon stock of the relevant area 1 (offset) land (in tonnes):
for pre-1990 forest land, the date on which it became pre-1990 forest land:
for area 2 (excess) land, the date on which it became area 2 (excess) land.
181V EPA to give information on request
The EPA must, on request, give a copy of the information on the register under section 181U about P90 offsetting land to the owner of that land or the relevant area 1 (offset) land.
The EPA must, on request, give the owner of pre-1990 forest land a statement of the number of units (if any) the owner would be liable to surrender under section 181E if that section were to apply in relation to the land.
In this section,—
owner, in relation to land, means a person who owns the land, or has previously owned it, or is a prospective transferee of it
request means a written request from the person to whom the information is to be given.
181W Regulations for P90 offsetting
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
prescribing the information to be included in, and other requirements for,—
P90 offset applications (section 181A):
P90 release criteria notices (section 181G):
applications to add land to area 2 (approved) land (section 181I):
prescribing re-use periods for area 2 (excess) land or P89 offsetting (excess) land (section 181B(2)﻿(b)﻿(iii) and (iv)):
prescribing additional criteria for approval of—
P90 offset applications (section 181B(2)﻿(h)):
applications to add land to area 2 (approved) land (section 181I):
prescribing additional P90 offset release criteria (section 181F(1)﻿(c)):
prescribing the methodology for determining—
baseline carbon stock (section 181F(2)):
expected carbon stock (section 181F(4)):
prescribing usual rotation periods for forest species (section 181F(6)):
providing for any other matters contemplated by this subpart, necessary for its administration, or necessary for giving it full effect.
See sections 3A and 3B for consultation requirements that apply to the making of the regulations.
The regulations come into force 3 months after the date of their publication under the Legislation Act 2019 or on any later date specified in the regulations.
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
 Subpart 4 — Post-1989 forest land (standard and permanent forestry)
182 Standard and permanent forestry on post-1989 forest land
In this subpart,—
final forestry emissions return means an emissions return that is prepared under section 183B and is not a provisional forestry emissions return
provisional forestry emissions return means an emissions return submitted under section 183.
To avoid doubt, standard forestry and permanent forestry comprise the same list of activities carried out in respect of post-1989 forest land, but the difference is that the person carrying out the activity has chosen the relevant Part of Schedule 4 to apply to the land.
