182A Conditions on registration as participant in certain activities of standard or permanent forestry in respect of post-1989 forest land
A person may not be registered as a participant under section 57 in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry that relates to—
owning any post-1989 forest land, unless the person is the landowner of the post-1989 forest land and—
there is no forestry right or lease registered in respect of that land; or
the person has the written agreement of any holder of a registered forestry right or registered lease in respect of that land to the person registering as a participant; or
holding a registered forestry right or being the leaseholder under a registered lease in respect of any post-1989 forest land, unless the person—
is the holder of the registered forestry right or the leaseholder of the registered lease; and
has the written agreement of the landowner of the land to the forestry right holder or leaseholder, as the case may be, registering as a participant.
A person may not be registered as a participant under section 57 in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in relation to exempt land that has been deforested 8 or less years ago unless the person—
has submitted an emissions return to the EPA that—
records the emissions from the deforestation of the land—
that would have been required to have been recorded in an annual emissions return under section 65, had the land not been declared to be exempt land; and
calculated in accordance with the methodology or methodologies prescribed for the deforestation activity listed in Part 1 of Schedule 3 that were applicable when the land was deforested; and
contains an assessment of the liability to surrender units that would have arisen in relation to the deforestation had the land not been declared to be exempt land; and
is accompanied by the prescribed fee (if any) and any other prescribed information; and
is signed by the person submitting the application; and
has surrendered, within 60 working days after the EPA gives the person a notice requiring the surrender, the number of units listed in the assessment under paragraph (a)﻿(ii); and
complies with subsection (1), if applicable.
To avoid doubt, if any person is registered as a participant carrying out an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in respect of any post-1989 forest land, no person (including that person) can be registered as a participant carrying out a different activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in respect of that land.
A person may not be registered as a participant under section 57 in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in relation to post-1989 forest land unless—
any action taken by the person in respect of the post-1989 forest land since 1 January 2008 (including, but not limited to, removal of any existing vegetation before planting of a forest species on the land) complied with the Resource Management Act 1991, including any plan under that Act, or the Forests Act 1949 that was in force at the time the action was taken; and
if the post-1989 forest land is subject to a pest management plan under the Biosecurity Act 1993 that imposes requirements in respect of any forest species on the land, the person has—
complied with the requirements; or
verified that any other person required to comply with the requirements has done so.
A person may not be registered as a participant under section 57 in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in relation to post-1989 forest land where the forest species on the land is predominantly naturally regenerated tree weeds unless the EPA is satisfied that the risk of tree weed spread from the land that is the subject of the application for registration is low.
Subsection (5) does not apply to any person who has registered as a participant before 1 January 2013.
182AB Restriction on registration as participant in relation to restricted forest land
A person may not be registered as a participant under section 57 in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in relation to restricted forest land, except in accordance with subpart 4A or 4B.
182B EPA to give public notice of criteria for assessing risk of tree weed spread
The EPA must give public notice of the criteria for assessing the risk of tree weed spread from land that is the subject of an application for registration under section 57.
182C Registration as participant in standard or permanent forestry
An application under section 57 to be registered as a participant in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry—
may be submitted for all post-1989 forest land in respect of which the applicant carries out the activity, or any part of the land in respect of which the applicant carries out the activity; and
must define the carbon accounting area or areas in respect of which the applicant wishes to be a participant; and
must be accompanied by a declaration, in the prescribed form, that—
any action taken by the applicant since 1 January 2008 in relation to the post-1989 forest land in respect of which the application is submitted (including, but not limited to, removal of any existing vegetation before planting of a forest species on the land) complied with the Resource Management Act 1991, including any plan under that Act or the Forests Act 1949 that was in force at the time the action was taken; and
if the post-1989 forest land is subject to a pest management plan under the Biosecurity Act 1993 that imposes requirements in respect of any forest species on the land, the applicant has—
complied with the requirements; or
verified that any other person required to comply with the requirements has done so; and
must be accompanied by any information prescribed by regulations made under this Act.
The EPA must keep the following records for the activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry for which a person is a participant (whether by registration under section 57 or otherwise):
the carbon accounting area or areas in respect of which the person is a participant; and
for each carbon accounting area used for an activity of standard forestry, whether or not it is a carbon accounting area (averaging); and
the unit balance of each carbon accounting area in respect of which the person is a participant, as calculated under the last emissions return submitted for the area.
A person who is a participant in standard forestry or permanent forestry (whether by registration under section 57 or otherwise) may apply to the EPA to add any carbon accounting area or areas to the post-1989 forest land in respect of which the person is recorded as a participant.
An application under subsection (3) must be—
in the prescribed form; and
accompanied by any prescribed fee and any prescribed information.
The EPA may (under this section) add a carbon accounting area to the post-1989 forest land in respect of which a person is recorded as a participant only if—
the EPA is satisfied that—
the person would (if appropriate) qualify to be registered as a participant in respect of the carbon accounting area under section 182A; and
the carbon accounting area—
is not restricted forest land; or
for restricted forest land, is permitted to be registered in accordance with subpart 4A or 4B; or
meets subsubparagraphs (A) and (B); and
where the forest species on that land is predominantly naturally regenerated tree weeds, the EPA is satisfied that the risk of tree weed spread from the land is low.
If an applicant identifies land in an application with reference to the land use capability class, the applicant must do so using only 1 of the following for the entire application:
the NZLRI map; or
a property-scale assessment undertaken in accordance with any methodology specified in regulations.
If the EPA—
registers a person as a participant under section 57 in relation to an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry, the EPA must notify the person under section 57(6):
receives an application to add a carbon accounting area and subsection (5) is satisfied, the EPA must—
update the participant's record to reflect the addition of the carbon accounting area; and
notify the participant accordingly.
The addition of a carbon accounting area under subsection (6)﻿(b)﻿(i) has effect on and after the date of the notice given under subsection (6)﻿(b)﻿(ii).
See also sections 182D and 182E (which require notice to the participant and notice to interested parties, if any).
182CA Further requirements for ETS application in respect of LUC class 1–6 unfarmed land
This section applies to an ETS application in respect of LUC class 1–6 land that is unfarmed land.
The application must, in addition to any other requirements, be accompanied by a statutory declaration in the prescribed form that, to the best of the applicant’s knowledge, the land is unfarmed land.
182CB Further requirements for ETS application in respect of mapped land other than LUC class 1–6 land
This section applies to an ETS application in respect of mapped land other than LUC class 1–6 land.
The application must, in addition to any other requirements, identify the land to which the application applies by reference to the applicable land use capability classification shown—
on the NZLRI map; or
in a property-scale assessment undertaken in accordance with any methodology specified in regulations.
182CC Further requirements for ETS application if property-scale assessment of land used
This section applies to an ETS application if—
this Act requires the applicant to identify the land use capability classification of the land to which the application applies; and
the applicant relies on a property-scale assessment of the land to identify the land use capability classification.
The application must, in addition to any other requirements, be accompanied by a statement by the person who undertook the property-scale assessment of the land that the assessment has been carried out in accordance with any methodology specified in regulations.
182CD Retention of property-scale assessment records
An applicant must keep records of property-scale assessment of the land use capability classification of land described in a record of title for 20 years if the applicant has used them in an ETS application.
182D Notice to forestry participant if their registration added or removed
The EPA must give written or electronic notice to a participant, or former participant, of the following matters as soon as practicable after the EPA carries them out under any of Parts 5 to 5D:
the participant’s registration or removal from registration in respect of an activity, and the date on which this took or takes effect:
the addition or removal of any area or land for which the participant is registered, and the date on which this took or takes effect.
182E Notice to interested party if forestry participant’s registration added or removed
A participant must notify the interested party (if any) of the following matters under this section, in writing or electronically, as soon as practicable after receiving the EPA’s notice about, or becoming aware of, the matter:
the participant’s registration, or removal from registration, in respect of an activity, and the date that this took or takes effect:
the addition or removal of any area or land for which the participant is registered, and the date that this took or takes effect.
The EPA must provide the participant with any address that it has recorded for the interested party.
In this section, interested party means—
the landowner, in relation to a participant who is registered for an activity relating to—
holding a registered forestry right or registered lease over land; or
being a party to a Crown conservation contract over land; or
any person with a registered forestry right or registered lease in respect of the land, in relation to a participant who is registered for an activity relating to owning post-1989 forest land.
182F Removing registration as participant in standard or permanent forestry
This section sets out some situations in which section 186 or 186B applies (which relate to ceasing participation for whole or part carbon accounting areas).
Section 186 applies if the EPA—
receives an application under section 58 for the removal of a person’s name from the register as a participant in standard forestry; or
is satisfied under section 59(2) that the person has ceased to carry out standard forestry or permanent forestry.
Section 186 applies if the EPA decides to remove the name of a person from the register in respect of an activity of standard forestry under section 59AA (for persistent non-compliance), or in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry under section 59A (because the person never carried out the activity).
A person who is a participant in standard forestry or permanent forestry—
may apply to the EPA to—
remove any carbon accounting area or areas from the post-1989 forest land in respect of which the person is recorded as a participant; or
remove post-1989 forest land from any carbon accounting area or areas in respect of which the person is recorded as a participant; and
must, as soon as practicable, notify the EPA if the person ceases to carry out the activity in respect of—
a carbon accounting area in respect of which the person is recorded as a participant; or
any land in a carbon accounting area in respect of which the person is recorded as a participant.
An application or a notice under subsection (4) must be—
in the prescribed form; and
accompanied by any prescribed fee and any prescribed information.
Section 186 applies if the EPA—
receives and approves an application to remove a carbon accounting area for which a person is recorded as a participant; or
receives a notice that a person has ceased to carry out standard forestry or permanent forestry on all of a carbon accounting area; or
is satisfied that a person has ceased to carry out standard forestry or permanent forestry on all of a carbon accounting area.
Section 186B applies if the EPA—
receives and approves an application to remove land from a carbon accounting area for which a person is recorded as a participant; or
receives a notice that a person has ceased to carry out standard forestry or permanent forestry on part of a carbon accounting area; or
is satisfied that a person has ceased to carry out standard forestry or permanent forestry on part of a carbon accounting area.
This section is subject to section 190A (which restricts the removal of land relating to permanent forestry).
182G Removing registration as participant in standard or permanent forestry in certain natural events or clearance for forest management
A person who is a participant in standard forestry or permanent forestry may, as soon as practicable, notify the EPA if all or part of the post-1989 forest land on which the person carries out the activity—
is affected by a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest on that land; or
is cleared land to which section 179A(1)﻿(c) applies (which is land cleared for best practice forest management that may not be treated as deforested).
The notice must—
include the prescribed information (if any); and
be signed by the person; and
be given—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
If the EPA is satisfied that the post-1989 forest land is land to which subsection (1)﻿(a) or (b) applies, then whichever of section 186 or 186B is relevant applies (so that the person is not liable to surrender units equal to the unit balance of the affected land).
182 Offsetting in relation to pre-1990 forest land
 Provisional and final forestry emissions returns
183 Provisional forestry emissions return in any year
This section applies to a person who is a participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry.
The person may, once before 1 July in each year, submit a provisional forestry emissions return prepared under section 183B for the activity—
that covers 1 or more of the carbon accounting areas for which the person is a participant in the activity (each a CAA1); and
that uses the last day of the previous calendar year as the relevant date.
183A Final forestry emissions return at end of mandatory emissions return period
This section applies to a person who is a participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry on the last day of a mandatory emissions return period.
The person must submit a final forestry emissions return prepared under section 183B for the activity—
that covers each carbon accounting area for which the person was a participant in the activity on the last day of the mandatory emissions return period (each a CAA1); and
that uses the last day of the mandatory emissions return period as the relevant date.
The deadline for submitting the emissions return is 6 months after the end of the mandatory emissions return period.
However, subsection (2) does not apply in relation to a carbon accounting area in relation to which a participant is not required to submit an emissions return under—
section 191G(b), relating to carbon accounting areas (averaging); or
section 193F(3)﻿(b), relating to temporary adverse event land.
183B Preparing provisional or final forestry emissions return
An emissions return prepared under this section must—
specify the CAA1s that the emissions return covers; and
specify the activity for which the person was a participant on the CAA1s; and
for each CAA1,—
specify the emissions return period that applies, by using subsection (4) and the relevant date from the provision that requires the return; and
specify the emissions and removals during the emissions return period; and
set out the calculation under section 184 of the person’s gross liability or entitlement for emissions and removals during the emissions return period; and
specify the person’s net liability or entitlement for emissions and removals during the emissions return period by,—
for a provisional forestry emissions return, specifying the same value as the person’s gross liability or entitlement; or
for a final forestry emissions return, setting out the calculation of that value under section 184A (which takes into account the liability or entitlement under each provisional forestry emissions return for an overlapping period, if any, and other matters); and
set out the calculation under section 184B of the unit balance; and
set out the calculation under section 184C of the person’s total liability or entitlement for all the CAA1s.
The emissions return must—
include the prescribed information (if any); and
be signed by the participant; and
when submitted under the relevant provision, be submitted—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
See section 62(1)﻿(b) and (c) for the requirements to calculate (and potentially verify) emissions and removals.
In this section, emissions return period, for a CAA1, means the period that—
starts on the latest of the following:
the first day of the mandatory emissions return period in which the relevant date falls:
if the CAA1 was constituted by registration under section 182C, the date on or before registration on which any of the land in the CAA1 became post-1989 forest land:
if the CAA1 was constituted in another way, the constitution date of the CAA1:
the day after the last day of the emissions return period for the CAA1 under,—
for a provisional forestry emissions return, the last provisional or final forestry emissions return submitted for the CAA1; and
for a final forestry emissions return, the last final forestry emissions return submitted for the CAA1; and
ends on the relevant date.
If subsection (4)﻿(a)﻿(ii) applies, the person must be treated as if they became a participant in respect of the CAA1 on the date under that subparagraph (before the CAA1 was actually constituted) for the purposes of calculating—
emissions and removals from the CAA1; and
the unit balance of the CAA1.
 Calculations for provisional and final forestry emissions returns

is the number of units required for removals from the CAA1 during the emissions return period

the g calculated under subsection (1) is a negative number, giving a gross liability; and
that gross liability is greater than the value p calculated for the CAA1 under section 184B,—

184A Net liability or entitlement for each CAA1 in final forestry emissions return
A person’s net liability or entitlement for a CAA1 over an emissions return period (h) is calculated as follows:
where—
is the person’s gross liability or entitlement for the CAA1 (under that same final forestry emissions return)

To avoid doubt, if there is no overlapping provisional forestry emissions return, a person’s net liability or entitlement is the same as their gross liability or entitlement for a CAA1.
However, if section 189F or 189G applies, h is recalculated as follows:

is the person’s net liability or entitlement calculated under subsection (1)

Finally, if—

In this section, overlapping provisional forestry emissions return means each provisional forestry emissions return (if any) submitted for a period that overlaps with the emissions return period of the final forestry emissions return.
184B Unit balance calculation for each CAA1 in emissions return
The unit balance of a CAA1 (u) is calculated for an emissions return as follows:
where—
is—
the previous unit balance of the CAA1 calculated under the last emissions return submitted for the CAA1; or
zero, if there is no such return
is the person’s net liability or entitlement for the CAA1 under the emissions return for which u is calculated.
184C Total liability or entitlement for all CAA1s in emissions return
A person’s total liability or entitlement for all the CAA1s covered by an emissions return is the sum of the person’s net liability or entitlement for each CAA1.
 Total liability or entitlement and unit balance has effect for all emissions returns
184D Total liability or entitlement has effect, and unit balance updated, when emissions return submitted
This section applies when a person submits a provisional or final forestry emissions return.
If the person’s total liability or entitlement for the CAA1s covered by the emissions return is—
a positive number, the person is entitled to receive (or be reimbursed) that number of New Zealand units; or
a negative number, the person is liable to surrender (or repay) that number of units.
For a final forestry emissions return, the person—
is entitled to be reimbursed (instead of to receive) units; or
is liable to repay (instead of to surrender) units—
to the extent that they surrendered, or received, more units for a CAA1 under provisional forestry emissions returns than required to satisfy their net liability or entitlement for the CAA1 under the final forestry emissions return.
The unit balance of each CAA1 covered by the emissions return is updated to the unit balance calculated under the return.
 New unit balance report for certain applications or notices
185 New unit balance report
A new unit balance report prepared under this section must—
specify the CAA2s that the report covers and, for each CAA2 whose boundaries are not the same as a CAA1, define the CAA2; and
specify the CAA1s (that are replaced by the CAA2s); and
set out the calculation under this section of the opening unit balance of each CAA2; and
if any CAA1 forms a notional CAA2 and a remainder CAA2 (because participation ceases for part of the CAA1 under section 186B), set out the calculation under this section of the person’s final liability or entitlement.
However, subsection (1) is subject to the following provisions (which limit reconfiguration):
section 191H (carbon accounting areas (averaging)):
section 192G (P89 offsetting (approved) land):
section 193H (temporary adverse event land).

 Maximum liability is unit balance of carbon accounting area
185A Maximum liability is unit balance of carbon accounting area
Despite section 63, a person who is or was a participant in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry is not liable to surrender more units in relation to any carbon accounting area or part of a carbon accounting area than the unit balance of that carbon accounting area or part of a carbon accounting area.
 Ceasing participation in standard or permanent forestry
186 Ceasing participation for whole carbon accounting areas
This section applies if a person ceases, or is to cease, participation in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry (on the end date) on 1 or more whole carbon accounting areas (each a CAA1).
However, this section does not apply if another provision of this Act requires an emissions return to be prepared for the situation.
To avoid doubt, this section applies whether—
the person is ceasing to be a participant in the activity in a CAA1, or is removing a CAA1 for which the person is recorded as a participant; or
the person is giving notice to the EPA, the EPA has approved an application from the person, or the EPA is acting under a provision of this Act; or
the CAA1s are some or all of the carbon accounting areas on which the person participates in the activity.
186A Effect of ceasing participation for whole carbon accounting areas
If section 186 applies, then, starting on the end date,—
the person ceases to be a participant in the activity in the CAA1s; and
the person is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the unit balance of each CAA1 (calculated under the last emissions return submitted for the CAA1).
However, subsection (1)﻿(b) does not apply if the person has ceased to be a participant because of section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest or land cleared for best practice forest management).
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
See sections 182D and 182E, which require notice to the participant and notice to interested parties, if any.
186B Ceasing participation for part carbon accounting areas
This section applies if a person ceases, or is to cease, participation in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry (on the end date) in only part of 1 or more carbon accounting areas (each a CAA1).
To avoid doubt, this section applies whether—
the person ceases to be a participant in the activity in part of a CAA1, or is removed from being recorded as a participant in respect of part of a CAA1; or
the person is giving notice to the EPA, the EPA has approved an application from the person, or the EPA is acting under a provision of this Act.
However, this section does not apply to a situation for which another provision of this Act already requires an emissions return to be prepared.
The person must—
prepare a final forestry emissions return under section 183B for the activity—
that covers each CAA1; and
that uses the end date as the relevant date; and
include in that return a new unit balance report under section 185 for the activity that covers the following carbon accounting areas (each a CAA2) formed from each CAA1:
a notional CAA2 for the part of the CAA1 where participation ceases:
a remainder CAA2 for the rest of the land in the CAA1.
The person must—
include the emissions return with the application or notice when it is made or given; or
if there is no application or notice, provide the emissions return when required by the EPA.
The land in a notional CAA2 must be treated as forest land if the person has ceased to be a participant because of section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest or land cleared for best practice forest management).
186C Effect of ceasing participation for part carbon accounting areas
This section applies if a final forestry emissions return (for the CAA1s) is provided to the EPA in accordance with section 186B, including a new unit balance report (for the CAA2s).
Starting on the end date,—
the emissions return is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA1s under section 184D); and
the person ceases to be a participant in the activity on the notional CAA2s; and
the person is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the opening unit balance calculated for each notional CAA2 in the new unit balance report; and
the person is a participant in the activity in the remainder CAA2s (instead of the CAA1s); and
the unit balance of each remainder CAA2 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report; but
any entitlement to receive units because of paragraph (a) is offset against any liability to surrender units under paragraph (c), so that the person’s final liability or entitlement is as calculated in the new unit balance report.
However, subsection (2)﻿(c) and (f) does not apply if the person has ceased to be a participant because of section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest or land cleared for best practice forest management).
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
See sections 182D and 182E, which require notice to the participant and notice to interested parties, if any.
186D If participant has never carried out activity in carbon accounting area
This section applies if the EPA is satisfied that a person is not carrying out, and has never carried out, the activity of standard or permanent forestry in a carbon accounting area, or part of an accounting area, for which they are registered.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to remove the person’s registration in respect of the carbon accounting area, or part carbon accounting area.
The person must surrender the unit balance that relates to the carbon accounting area (or part area).
At least 60 days before amending the register, the EPA must notify the person—
that the EPA proposes to remove the person’s registration in respect of the carbon accounting area, or part carbon accounting area; and
of the reason for the proposed removal; and
of the actions that the person may take to prevent the removal (for example, provide evidence that the person carries out the activity in the carbon accounting area).
The EPA may still take action under this section if it is unable to notify the person of its proposal to do so because it is not reasonably practicable to locate them or their address.
186E Deforesting pre-1990 offsetting forest land before usual rotation period of forest species on pre-1990 forest land
186F Regulations relating to offsetting
186G EPA may revoke approval in certain circumstances
186H Treatment of allocations in respect of pre-1990 forest land that is offset
186I Participant in respect of pre-1990 offsetting forest land
186J Methodology for pre-1990 offsetting forest land cleared after usual rotation period is completed
 Transmission of interest relating to standard or permanent forestry
187 Transmission of interest in post-1989 forest land
This section applies—
if, subject to section 157A(4), a person registered as a participant in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry and who is described in the first column of Part A of the following table transfers, including by way of sale, assignment, or by operation of law, all or any of the interest described in the second column of Part A of the table to a person described in the third column of Part A of the table:
if a person registered as a participant in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry and who is described in the first column of Part B of the following table grants an interest or enters into a contract described in the second column of Part B of the table:
if an interest described in the second column of Part C of the following table expires or is terminated, and the person described in the first column of Part C of the table is, in relation to that interest, registered as a participant in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry:
Despite subsection (1)﻿(b), if a transferor covered by that paragraph grants a registered forestry right or registered lease described in the second column of Part B of the table in that subsection, this section applies only if, before the date of transmission,—
the transferor and the transferee have agreed in writing that the transferee is to become the participant in relation to the post-1989 forest land to which the transmitted interest relates; and
the transferor has given written notice of the agreement to the EPA.
In this section and section 187A,—
CAA1—
means a carbon accounting area that contains post-1989 forest land to which a transmitted interest relates; and
includes, where a transmitted interest relates to post-1989 forest land in part of a carbon accounting area, that carbon accounting area:
each of the persons described in the first column of the table in subsection (1) is a transferor:
each of the persons described in the third column of the table in subsection (1) is a transferee:
transmitted interest means,—
in the circumstances described in subsection (1)﻿(a), the post-1989 forest land, registered forestry right over post-1989 forest land, registered lease of post-1989 forest land, or Crown conservation contract that is transferred:
in the circumstances described in subsection (1)﻿(b), the registered forestry right over post-1989 forest land, registered lease of post-1989 forest land, or Crown conservation contract that is granted or entered into:
in the circumstances described in subsection (1)﻿(c), the interest in the registered forestry right over post-1989 forest land, registered lease of post-1989 forest land, or Crown conservation contract that has expired or been terminated:
date of transmission means,—
in the circumstances described in subsection (1)﻿(a), the date of transfer of—
the post-1989 forest land:
the registered forestry right over post-1989 forest land:
the registered lease of post-1989 forest land:
the Crown conservation contract:
in the circumstances described in subsection (1)﻿(b), the date of registration of the registered forestry right over post-1989 forest land, the date of registration of the registered lease of post-1989 forest land, or the date the Crown conservation contract is entered into:
in the circumstances described in subsection (1)﻿(c), the date that the registered forestry right over post-1989 forest land, registered lease of post-1989 forest land, or Crown conservation contract expires or is terminated.
The transferor and transferee must give notice of the transmission to the EPA—
within 20 working days of the date of transmission; or
if the transmission occurred by operation of law, as soon as practicable after the date of transmission.
The notice must—
include a final forestry emissions return prepared by the transferor under section 183B for the activity—
that covers each CAA1; and
that uses the date of transmission as the relevant date; and
include in that return a new unit balance report prepared by the transferor under section 185 for the activity that covers the following carbon accounting areas (each a CAA2):
for each CAA1 where the transmitted interest applies to its entire area, a transferee CAA2 with the same boundaries as the CAA1:
for each other CAA1,—
a transferee CAA2 for the part of the CAA1 to which the transmitted interest relates; and
a transferor CAA2 for the rest of the CAA1.
The notice must be—
in the prescribed form; and
accompanied by any prescribed fees or charges and any prescribed information; and
signed by both the transferor and the transferee.
However, if the transmitted interest is part of a deceased participant’s estate,—
for the transfer to the executor or administrator,—
subsections (4) to (6) do not apply (so that no notice, final forestry emissions return, or new unit balance report is required); but
section 187A(2)﻿(b) and (c) and (3) still applies; and
for the transfer from the executor or administrator to a successor,—
the transferee (not the transferor) must prepare the final forestry emissions return and new unit balance report required by subsection (5); and
for the purposes of those documents, the CAA1s are the CAA1s that relate to the transfer to the executor or administrator; and
in every case, the executor or administrator of more than 1 deceased participant’s estate is treated under this Act as if they were a separate participant for each of those estates.
To avoid doubt,—
for the purposes of section 54(4), but subject to section 187D, a transferor continues to be liable in respect of any obligations that arose in relation to the CAA1 while the transferor was a participant in respect of the post-1989 forest land to which the transmitted interest relates (for example, in respect of the submitting of returns and surrendering of units); and
a transferor is not required to notify the EPA separately under section 59 if the result of the transfer is that the transferor is ceasing to carry out the activity; and
the EPA is not required to notify any person under section 182C(6)﻿(a) of the registration of the transferee under section 57 if that registration is in accordance with this section.
187A Effect of transmission of interest in post-1989 forest land
This section applies if notice of a transmission is given to the EPA in accordance with section 187, including a final forestry emissions return (for the CAA1s) and a new unit balance report (for the CAA2s).
Starting on the date of transmission,—
the emissions return is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA1s under section 184D); and
the transferee becomes a participant on the transferee CAA2s in the relevant activity referred to in the fourth column of the table in section 187(1); and
the transferor,—
if there is 1 or more transferor CAA2s, is a participant in the relevant activity described in section 187(1) on the transferor CAA2s (instead of the CAA1s); or
otherwise, ceases to be a participant in that activity on the CAA1s; and
the unit balance of each CAA2 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
 Non-compliance for transmitted interests
187B EPA may act if person fails to give notice of transmitted interest
This section applies if—
the EPA is satisfied that a notice has not been given in accordance with section 187(4) (for a transmission of interest); and
section 187D (for 1 or more transmissions of interest after that first one) does not apply to the transmission.
In particular, this section applies to whichever of the following notices (forming part of that overall notice required by section 187(4)) has not been given in accordance with that provision (each a failed notice):
the part that requires information from the transferor (the transferor notice):
the part that requires information from the transferee (the transferee notice).
The EPA may act under section 187C(2) and (3) in relation to a failed notice if—
the EPA notifies the transferor or transferee of its intention to do so; and
the EPA specifies the following deadline for them to give or correct the required notice: the end of the 90th working day after the EPA gives its notice; and
they do not give or correct the required notice by the deadline.
The EPA may act under 1 or both of section 187C(2) and (3) and section 187C(4) and (5) in relation to a failed notice if—
the following requirements are met:
the EPA notifies the transferor or transferee of its intention to do so; and
the EPA specifies the following deadline for them to give or correct the required notice:
6 months after the end of the mandatory emissions return period in which the date of transmission falls; or
if the EPA gives its notice after the deadline in subsubparagraph (A), the end of the 90th working day after the EPA gives its notice; and
they do not give or correct the required notice by the deadline; or
the EPA is unable to notify the transferor or transferee of its intention to do so because it is not reasonably practicable to identify or locate them or their address.
The EPA may act under section 187C(6) and (7) in relation to a failed notice if—
the following requirements are met:
the EPA notifies the transferor or transferee of its intention to do so; and
the EPA specifies the following deadline for them to give or correct the required notice:
6 months after the end of the mandatory emissions return period in which the date of transmission falls; or
if the EPA gives its notice after the deadline in subsubparagraph (A), the end of the 90th working day after the EPA gives its notice; and
they do not give or correct the required notice by the deadline; or
the EPA is unable to notify the transferor or transferee of its intention to do so because it is not reasonably practicable to identify or locate them or their address.
However, where this section also applies to 1 or more other transmissions of interest for different parts of the same CAA1, the EPA must not act under section 187C(6) and (7) unless it is authorised to do so (by satisfying subsection (5) of this section) in respect of both the transferor and transferee of all of the transmissions.
If the transferor or transferee gives or corrects the required notice by the deadline specified in a notice given by the EPA under this section, their notice must be treated as having been given to the EPA—
in accordance with section 187(4); and
on the last day on which it could have been given under that provision.
187C How EPA may act
In acting under this section in relation to a failed notice, the EPA may—
use subsections (2) and (3) to correct matters:
use subsections (4) and (5) to remove registration in relation to relevant CAA2s (after the transmission is registered):
use subsections (6) and (7) to remove registration in relation to relevant CAA1s (where the transmission is not registered).
The EPA may,—
if the failed notice has not been given, prepare the notice that ought to have been given; or
if the failed notice has been given but is not complete, complete the notice.
The EPA may do the following when preparing or completing the notice:
if the notice must include an emissions return (including any new unit balance report), the EPA may apply—
section 120 to amend an emissions return that was included; or
section 121 to assess the matters that should have been in an emissions return that was not included; and
if the notice must include any other information, the EPA may prepare or complete that information by making any required assumptions or estimates.
If the transmission of interest has had effect in accordance with section 187A, the EPA may amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record that—
the transferee ceased to be a participant in the relevant activity on each transferee CAA2 immediately after becoming a participant for those, if the transferee notice is a failed notice:
the transferor ceased to be a participant in the relevant activity on the transferor CAA2s immediately after becoming a participant for those, if—
there are 1 or more transferor CAA2s; and
the transferor notice is a failed notice.
If the EPA acts under subsection (4),—
the person who ceases to be a participant on certain CAA2s is liable to surrender the number of New Zealand units equal to the unit balance of each of those CAA2s (calculated under the last emissions return submitted for the CAA2); and
see sections 182D and 182E, which require notice to the participant and notice to interested parties, if any.
If the transmission of interest has not had effect in accordance with section 187A, the EPA may amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record that the transferor ceased to be a participant in the relevant activity in the CAA1s on the date of transmission.
If the EPA acts under subsection (6),—
the transferor is liable to surrender the number of New Zealand units equal to the unit balance of each of those CAA1s (calculated under the last emissions return submitted for the CAA1); and
see sections 182D and 182E, which require notice to the participant and notice to interested parties, if any.
187D EPA may attribute liability and entitlement to final transferee after earlier non-compliant transmission
This section applies if a person would have been a transferee of a transmitted interest (the final transmission) under section 187 had it not been for—
the failure of any person to give notice in accordance with section 187(4) for an earlier transmission of interest (the first transmission); and
if there were 1 or more other transmissions of interest between the first and final transmissions, those other transmissions not becoming subject to (and compliant with) the requirements of section 187 after that failure.
The EPA must treat the transferee of the final transmission as if they were liable to surrender units for all emissions, and entitled to receive units for all removals, from the land in their transferee CAA2s in the period that—
starts immediately after the emissions return period of the last emissions return submitted for the CAA1s of the first transmission; and
ends on the date of transmission of the final transmission.
The EPA must do so by acting under section 187C(2) to (5), which applies with any required modifications, to the extent required to—
calculate that liability and entitlement; and
amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record that the transferee is a participant in the relevant activity on the transferee CAA2s.
The EPA must also act under section 187C(2) to (5), which applies with any required modifications, to the extent required to do 1 or both of the following for any transferor CAA2s that derive, during the first or any other transmission, from the land in the CAA1s of the first transmission:
calculate the liability and entitlement of the transferor of the transferor CAA2:
amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record that the transferor is a participant in the relevant activity in the transferor CAA2.
 Application to reconfigure carbon accounting areas for standard or permanent forestry
188 Application to reconfigure carbon accounting areas for standard or permanent forestry
A participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry may apply to reconfigure any of the carbon accounting areas for the activity.
The application must—
specify the activity; and
specify the land to which the application relates, which must be 1 or more whole carbon accounting areas for the activity (each a CAA1); and
include a final forestry emissions return prepared under section 183B for the activity—
that covers the CAA1s; and
that uses the date on which the application is submitted to the EPA as the relevant date; and
include in that return a new unit balance report prepared under section 185 for the activity that covers 1 or more carbon accounting areas (CAA2s) consisting of all the same land in the CAA1s.
The application must also—
be signed by the applicant; and
be submitted—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
However, subsection (1) is subject to the following provisions (which limit reconfiguration):
section 191H (carbon accounting areas (averaging)):
section 192G (P89 offsetting (approved) land):
section 193H (temporary adverse event land).
188A Criteria to reconfigure carbon accounting areas for standard or permanent forestry
If a person submits an application under section 188 (for a participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry to reconfigure carbon accounting areas for the activity), the EPA,—
if satisfied that the criteria in subsection (2) are met, must approve the application; or
otherwise, may decline the application.
The criteria are—
that the application complies with section 188; and
that the applicant has paid any prescribed fees or charges; and
that any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 168(1)﻿(naa) are met.
In considering the application, the EPA must treat the land to which it relates as post-1989 forest land.
188B Approval of application to reconfigure carbon accounting areas for standard or permanent forestry
This section applies if the EPA approves a person’s application under section 188 (for a participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry to reconfigure carbon accounting areas for the activity).
Starting on the day on which the application was submitted to the EPA,—
the emissions return for the CAA1s is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA1s under section 184D); and
the person is a participant in the activity on the CAA2s (instead of the CAA1s); and
the person is not liable to surrender the unit balance of each CAA1; and
the unit balance of each CAA2 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
188C Restriction start date of reconfigured carbon accounting area for permanent forestry
This section applies if a person reconfigures carbon accounting areas for permanent forestry by approval of an application under section 188.
For the purposes of section 190, the restriction start date of a CAA2 is the latest restriction start date of the CAA1s that overlap with the CAA2 (because any land in the CAA1 became land in the CAA2).
 Application to change activity on post-1989 forest land
189 Application to change activity on post-1989 forest land
A participant in an initial activity on any post-1989 forest land may apply to become a participant in—
a final activity on the land, if the initial activity is standard forestry or permanent forestry; or
1 or more final activities on the land, if the initial activity is PFSI activity.
To avoid doubt, a change in activity carries over the unit balance of each carbon accounting area (or the net number of units transferred for PFSI land) from the initial activity to the final activity.
If the initial activity is standard forestry or permanent forestry, the application must—
specify the initial activity and the final activity; and
specify the land to which the application relates, which must be 1 or more whole carbon accounting areas for the initial activity (each a CAA1); and
include a final forestry emissions return prepared under section 183B for the initial activity—
that covers the CAA1s; and
that uses the date on which the application is submitted to the EPA as the relevant date; and
that includes any liability required to be included by section 189F(4) or 189G(4); and
include in that return a new unit balance report prepared under section 185 for the final activity that covers the following 1 or more carbon accounting areas (each a CAA2):
CAA2s that have the same boundaries as the CAA1s, to the extent that subparagraph (ii) does not apply:
if the clear-fell exception applies and any land that is now in 1 or more CAA1s was clear-felled after the forest sink covenant was terminated,—
a CAA2 for all of the land that was clear-felled; and
a CAA2 for each CAA1 to the extent it was not clear-felled.
If the initial activity is PFSI activity, the application must—
specify the initial activity and the 1 or more final activities; and
specify the land to which the application relates (the PFSI land), which must be all of the forest land that a forest sink covenant is registered against; and
include an emissions return prepared under section 189C for the initial activity that covers the PFSI land, and specifying the liability (if any) calculated under section 189E; and
include in that return a new unit balance report prepared under section 189D for each final activity that covers the following carbon accounting areas (each a CAA2):
if the final activity for any of the land is standard forestry, one CAA2 for all of that land:
if the final activity for any of the land is permanent forestry, one CAA2 for all of that land, unless subparagraph (iii) applies:
if the final activity for any of the land is permanent forestry (the PF land) and any of the PF land was clear-felled after the forest sink covenant was terminated,—
one CAA2 for all of the PF land that was clear-felled; and
one CAA2 for the rest of the PF land.
The application must also—
be signed by the applicant; and
be submitted—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
The following table specifies the matters referred to in this section (under the relevant headings):
As indicated in the table, the clear-fell exception applies to—
a change from PFSI activity (initial activity) to permanent forestry (final activity):
a change from standard forestry (initial activity) to permanent forestry (final activity), if the activity on the land was previously changed from PFSI activity (previous activity) to standard forestry under this section.
189A Criteria to change activity on post-1989 forest land
If a person submits an application under section 189 (for a participant in an initial activity on post-1989 forest land to become a participant in a final activity on the land), the EPA,—
if satisfied that the criteria in subsection (2) are met, must approve the application; or
otherwise, may decline the application.
The criteria are—
that the application complies with section 189; and
that the applicant has paid any prescribed fees or charges; and
if the initial activity is PFSI activity, that the EPA is satisfied that the person would (if appropriate) qualify to be registered as a participant in respect of the land under section 182A; and
that any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 168(1)﻿(naa) are met.
In considering the application,—
if the initial activity is standard forestry or permanent forestry, the EPA must treat the land to which the application relates as post-1989 forest land; or
if the initial activity is PFSI activity, the EPA must treat the forest land to which the application relates as post-1989 forest land.
189B Approval of application to change activity on post-1989 forest land
This section applies if the EPA approves a person’s application under section 189 (for a participant in an initial activity on post-1989 forest land to become a participant in a final activity on the land).
If the initial activity is standard forestry or permanent forestry, then, starting on the day on which the application was submitted to the EPA,—
the emissions return for the CAA1s is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA1s under section 184D); and
the person ceases to be a participant in the initial activity on the CAA1s; and
the person becomes a participant in the final activity on the CAA2s; and
the person is not liable to surrender the unit balance of each CAA1; and
the unit balance of each CAA2 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report.
If the initial activity is PFSI activity, then, starting on the day on which the application was submitted to the EPA,—
the forest sink covenant registered against the PFSI land is terminated; and
if section 189E applies to a CAA2, the person is liable to surrender the number of units (if any) calculated under that section and specified in the emissions return; and
for each CAA2, the person becomes a participant in the final activity specified for that CAA2 in the application; and
the unit balance of each CAA2 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.

in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and

189D New unit balance reports for application to change from PFSI activity
A new unit balance report prepared under this section for a final activity must—
specify the CAA2 that the report covers or, if the final activity is permanent forestry to which section 189(4)﻿(d)﻿(iii) applies, specify and define each CAA2 that the report covers; and

189E Liability to surrender units on transfer from PFSI activity to standard forestry in carbon accounting area (averaging)
This section applies to a CAA2 if—
a person submits an application under section 189; and

where—
is the determined carbon stock of area A, determined as if it were in a carbon accounting area (averaging) (in tonnes per hectare)

is the area (in hectares) of area A.

The liability to surrender units under this section—
is to be treated as a liability for emissions from the PFSI land; and
must be included in the emissions return under section 189(4)﻿(c) as the person’s liability for the land.
In this section, terms defined in section 191 have the meanings given in that section.

a person submits an application under section 189; and
the initial activity is permanent forestry and the final activity is standard forestry; and
