 Restrictions for permanent forestry land
190 Permanent forestry period for land
If a person becomes registered as a participant carrying out permanent forestry in respect of any land, the permanent forestry period for the land is—
an initial period of 50 years starting on the restriction start date; and
any 1 or more consecutive periods of a further 25 years for which a participant chooses the option under section 190J(1)﻿(a).
The restriction start date for the land is specified in column 4 of the table, which applies to a situation as follows:
if no initial activity is specified (in column 2), it means the person became registered for the permanent forestry without reference to any initial activity on the land:
if an initial activity is specified (in column 2) and no previous activity is specified (in column 1), it means—
the person became registered for the permanent forestry by acceptance of an application under section 189 to change from the initial activity; and
the person previously became registered for the initial activity without reference to any previous activity:
if an initial activity is specified (in column 2) and a previous activity is specified (in column 1), it means—
the person became registered for the permanent forestry by acceptance of an application under section 189 to change from the initial activity; and
the person previously became registered for the initial activity by acceptance of an application under section 189 to change from the previous activity.
However, as indicated in column 5 of the table, the restriction start date is the registration date for the CAA2 formed from clear-felled land if—
the clear-fell exception applied in the application under section 189 to change from the initial activity; and
any of the land in the application was clear-felled after the forest sink covenant was terminated.
After any land’s permanent forestry period has started, its permanent forestry period—
may change under section 188C (if carbon accounting areas are reconfigured); but
does not change if the land becomes part of a new carbon accounting area when—
a person ceases to be a participant on other land because of section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest or land cleared for best practice forest management); or
an interest is transmitted under section 187; or
other land is removed in accordance with section 190B (an exception requiring the Minister’s approval); or
any of the land in the carbon accounting area becomes temporary adverse event land under section 193C.
In this section,—
covenant date means the date of registration of the forest sink covenant on land
registration date means the date on which the person became registered as a participant in permanent forestry on the land.
The following table contains the columns referred to in this section:
190A Restriction on ceasing to be registered for permanent forestry
The only ways in which a person may cease to be registered as a participant carrying out permanent forestry in respect of any land are as follows:
the person is exempted from this section by an Order in Council under section 60A:
the person ceases to be a participant because of—
section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest or land cleared for best practice forest management); or
section 193L(2)﻿(e) (for temporary adverse event land that becomes permanently affected land):
the land becomes land for which a transferee under section 187 is instead registered as carrying out permanent forestry (if there is a transmitted interest):
the registration is removed in accordance with section 190B (an exception requiring the Minister’s approval):
the registration for the whole carbon accounting area that includes the land is removed because of section 190G (after land is deforested):
after the permanent forestry period ends,—
the EPA removes the registration under section 190K (because the person chooses that option, for example):
the person changes from permanent forestry to standard forestry on the land by application under section 189.
This section overrides any other provision of this Act.
190B Minister may approve removal of land from permanent forestry
This section sets out an exception by which a person can cease to be registered as a participant carrying out permanent forestry in respect of any land (the removal of land), whether all or part of a carbon accounting area.
The person must—
first obtain the Minister’s approval in writing to the removal of land; and
then apply for the removal of land under section 182F(4)﻿(a)﻿(i) or (ii) and comply with sections 186 and 186A or sections 186B and 186C (whichever apply).
The provisions referred to in subsection (2)﻿(b), and the provisions applied by them, apply as if the land subject to the removal of land were forest land.
The Minister may approve the removal of land only to the extent that the Minister is satisfied that—
it would be unreasonable in the circumstances to require the person to remain registered in respect of the land; and
the removal will not materially undermine the environmental integrity of 1 or both of the following:
the activity of permanent forestry as a whole (not just by that person):
the emissions trading scheme.
In considering those matters, the Minister must have regard to—
the desirability of minimising any compliance and administrative costs associated with the emissions trading scheme; and
the relative costs of approving or not approving the removal of land, and who bears the costs; and
any other matters the Minister considers relevant.
190C Exception from prohibition on clear-felling and deforestation
Sections 190D to 190H do not apply to—
land for which a person ceases to be a participant because of—
section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest or land cleared for best practice forest management); or
section 193L(2)﻿(e) (for temporary adverse event land that becomes permanently affected land); or
temporary adverse event land.
However, if land ceases to be temporary adverse event land and section 193Q applies, sections 190D to 190H do apply to the land.
For that purpose,—
any clear-felling or deforestation that occurred while the land was temporary adverse event land is to be treated as having occurred on the date the land ceased to be temporary adverse event land; but
any penalty must be calculated by reference to the pre-event carbon stock rate for the land under section 193A(3)﻿(d)﻿(i).
190D Permanent forestry land must not be clear-felled
A person who is registered as a participant carrying out permanent forestry on any land must ensure that the land is not clear-felled.
If any of the land is clear-felled (the clear-felled land),—
the person must, as soon as practicable, notify the EPA of the clear-felling; and
the EPA must apply section 190E (pecuniary penalty for clear-felling) when required by that section.
190E Pecuniary penalty for clear-felling of permanent forestry land
This section applies after—
a person has notified the EPA of clear-felling under section 190D(2); and
a final forestry emissions return has been submitted that covers the 1 or more carbon accounting areas that include the clear-felled land.
The EPA must apply to the court for a pecuniary penalty order against the person for contravening section 190D(1) unless the EPA is satisfied that the defence applies.
The court—
must determine whether the person has contravened section 190D(1); and
must determine whether the person has established, on the balance of probabilities, that the defence applies; and
if it is satisfied that the person has contravened the provision without a defence,—
must make a declaration of contravention; and
must order the person to pay a pecuniary penalty to the Crown.
The amount of the pecuniary penalty—
must be the deemed value of the part of the forest on the clear-felled land that was cleared or killed below the requirement for tree crown cover from forest species of 30% in each hectare, as determined in accordance with regulations; but
may be reduced, at the court’s discretion, if the court is satisfied that the person has a reasonable excuse for the contravention.
In this section, defence means that—
the clear-felling was beyond the person’s control; and
the person could not reasonably have foreseen the clear-felling; and
the person could not reasonably have taken steps to prevent the clear-felling.
190F Regulations for pecuniary penalty for clear-felling
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes in relation to a pecuniary penalty under section 190E:
setting out how to calculate the deemed value of the part of the forest on clear-felled land that was cleared or killed below the requirement for tree crown cover from forest species of 30% in each hectare:
specifying different factors that affect the calculation of the deemed value, for example,—
the area of the clear-felled land, in hectares:
the geographic region of the clear-felled land:
the forest species, or the type of forest, that was on the clear-felled land:
the age or size of the forest that was on the clear-felled land:
providing for any other matters contemplated by section 190E, necessary for its administration, or necessary for giving it full effect.
Before recommending the making of regulations under this section, the Minister must—
consider—
the differences in value between forests of different types or ages or with trees of different forest species or sizes; and
the market value of the wood and other products removed from forests, and the historic variation in the market value; and
the need to assign an appropriate value for forests with no market, or for which no market price is available, so as to deter clear-felling on all land in permanent forestry; and
any need to deem the volume of the harvest from a forest; and
comply with the consultation requirements in sections 3A and 3B.
Regulations made under this section come into force 3 months after the date of their publication under the Legislation Act 2019, or on any later date specified in the regulations.
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
190G Permanent forestry land must not be deforested
A person who is registered as a participant carrying out permanent forestry on any land must ensure that the land is not deforested.
If any of the land is deforested (the deforested land),—
the person ceases to be a participant in permanent forestry in respect of each carbon accounting area that includes any deforested land (each a CAA1); and
accordingly,—
the person must notify the EPA under section 182F(4)﻿(b) that they have ceased to carry out the activity on the CAA1s; and
sections 186 and 186A apply in respect of the CAA1s; and
the EPA must apply section 190H (pecuniary penalty for deforestation).
190H Pecuniary penalty for deforestation of permanent forestry land
If this section applies, the EPA must apply to the court for a pecuniary penalty order against the person for contravening section 190G(1) unless the EPA is satisfied that the defence applies.
The court—
must determine whether the person has contravened section 190G(1); and
must determine whether the person has established, on the balance of probabilities, that the defence applies; and
if it is satisfied that the person has contravened the provision without a defence,—
must make a declaration of contravention; and
must order the person to pay a pecuniary penalty to the Crown.
The amount of the pecuniary penalty, in dollars (a), must be calculated as follows:
where—
is the number of units equal to the sum of the unit balance of each CAA1 that was calculated under the last emissions return submitted for the CAA1 before the clearing that caused the deforestation
is the price, in dollars, of carbon per tonne on the final date on which deforestation occurred, as set by or in accordance with regulations made under section 30W.
However, the amount may be reduced, at the court’s discretion, if the court is satisfied that the person has a reasonable excuse for the contravention.
In this section, defence means that—
the deforestation was beyond the person’s control; and
the person could not reasonably have foreseen the deforestation; and
the person could not reasonably have taken steps to prevent the deforestation.
190I Due dates for payment of penalties and recovery of EPA’s costs
This section applies if the court orders that a person pay a pecuniary penalty under section 190E or 190H.
The court must also order that the penalty be applied first to pay the EPA’s actual costs in bringing the proceedings.
The person must pay the penalty—
within 20 working days after the date on which the order is made, or by any later date specified by the order; or
by the date or dates agreed under a deferred payment arrangement under section 135A.
190J Option must be chosen at end of permanent forestry period
After the permanent forestry period ends, the participant carrying out permanent forestry on post-1989 forest land must choose an option for each carbon accounting area (each a CAA1) by doing 1 of the following:
giving notice to the EPA that they will carry out permanent forestry on the CAA1 for a further 25 years; or
removing the CAA1 from permanent forestry by any means available under this Act.
The participant must choose an option before or when the first of the following documents is submitted for the CAA1:
a provisional forestry emissions return:
a final forestry emissions return under section 183A for the mandatory emissions return period in which the permanent forestry period ended.
If the participant does not choose an option before or when submitting an emissions return described by subsection (2), or does not submit the final forestry emissions return described by subsection (2), the EPA must give notice to the participant.
The EPA’s notice must state—
that the participant must choose an option for each CAA1 within 30 working days after the EPA gave its notice; and
that a CAA1 will be removed from permanent forestry if the participant does not choose an option for it by then.
190K Removal of carbon accounting area from permanent forestry
This section applies to a CAA1 if the participant does not choose an option for the CAA1 by the deadline in the EPA’s notice given under section 190J(3).
As a result,—
the participant ceases to be a participant in permanent forestry on the CAA1; and
the participant is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the unit balance of the CAA1 (calculated under the last emissions return submitted for the CAA1).
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
 Subpart 4A — The 25% allowance for restricted forest land
190KA Further requirements for registration as participant in relation to restricted forest land
A person may apply to be registered under section 57 as a participant in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in relation to restricted forest land relying on the 25% allowance.
A person who is a participant in standard forestry or permanent forestry may apply to the EPA to add any carbon accounting area or areas that include restricted forest land relying on the 25% allowance.
An application under subsection (1) or (2) must, in addition to any other requirements,—
identify the record or records of title that comprise the individual farm to which the application applies; and
identify the restricted forest land for each individual farm to which the application applies by reference to the applicable land use capability class shown—
on the NZLRI map; or
in a property-scale assessment undertaken in accordance with any methodology specified in regulations; and
be accompanied by a statutory declaration made by the applicant, in the prescribed form, that the restricted forest land identified has not knowingly been submitted relying on the 25% allowance before, unless—
the application is submitted in accordance with section 190KB; or
any earlier application that relied on the 25% allowance was not accepted.
If an application under subsection (1) or (2) is made in respect of an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry that relates to holding a registered forestry right or being the leaseholder under a registered lease, the application must also include the written consent of the landowner, which must state—
the record or records of title for the individual farm to which the landowner’s consent relates; and
that the landowner consents to the inclusion of that land in the 25% allowance for that individual farm.
The EPA may register a person as a participant under section 57 in relation to an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry relying on the 25% allowance only if the EPA is satisfied that the registration would not exceed the 25% allowance for the individual farm.
The EPA may update a participant’s record to reflect the addition of a carbon accounting area relying on the 25% allowance only if the EPA is satisfied that updating the record would not exceed the 25% allowance for the individual farm.
If the EPA has accepted an application under subsection (1) or (2), the records of title that make up the individual farm that was the subject of that application may not be varied, or included in an application under this section in relation to any other individual farm.
190KB More than one 25% allowance application permitted
A person may make more than 1 application under section 190KA unless—
the combined area of restricted forest land identified in all the applications for registration would exceed the 25% allowance; or
since the first application in respect of an individual farm,—
the ownership of the record or records of title has changed; or
the farm boundary has changed.
190KC Notification of 25% allowance to be recorded against record of title
If the EPA registers a person as a participant under section 57 in relation to an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry relying on the 25% allowance, the EPA must—
notify the Registrar-General that the registration relying on the 25% allowance has occurred in respect of the individual farm; and
include in the notification the details of each record of title comprising the individual farm.
If the EPA updates a participant’s record to include a carbon accounting area relying on the 25% allowance, the EPA must—
notify the Registrar-General that the update relying on the 25% allowance has occurred in respect of the individual farm; and
include in the notification the details of each record of title comprising the individual farm.
The Registrar-General must record a notice under subsection (1) or (2) against the record or records of title of the individual farm.
If land described in a record of title is subdivided and the record of title has a notice under this section recorded against it, the notice must be recorded against all records of title resulting from the subdivision.
If land described in a record of title is merged with another record of title and either record of title has a notice under this section recorded against it, the notice or notices must be recorded against the merged record of title.
190KD The 25% allowance is in addition to LUC class 6 land permit
The 25% allowance is in addition to any LUC class 6 land that may be registered using a LUC class 6 land permit issued in respect of the same individual farm.
190KE The 25% allowance is not increased if LUC class 1–6 land area increases
If the landowner of an individual farm becomes the landowner of LUC class 1–6 land adjacent to the individual farm, the 25% allowance for the individual farm does not increase.
 Subpart 4B — LUC class 6 land permits
 Application of this subpart
190KF Application of this subpart
This subpart applies to LUC class 6 land.
 Ballots for LUC class 6 land permits
190KG Permit allocation in addition to 25% allowance
The number of hectares of LUC class 6 land that may be registered under a LUC class 6 land permit is in addition to any LUC class 6 land registered under the 25% allowance available to the permit holder under subpart 4A in relation to LUC class 6 land on the same individual farm.
190KH Annual hectare limit
The annual hectare limit of LUC class 6 land in total for the first and second ballots in a calendar year is,—
if no regulations are made under section 190KZE, 15,000 hectares; or
if regulations are made under section 190KZE, the limit specified in those regulations.
190KI Ballots for LUC class 6 land permits
Each calendar year, the EPA must issue LUC class 6 land permits by conducting 2 ballots: the first ballot and the second ballot.
Each ballot must—
be conducted so as to provide the prescribed amount of hectares that is reserved for small applications; and
be completed within any prescribed time frames.
A small application that is not drawn from a ballot before the ballot’s reserve of hectares for small applications is fully used is included in that ballot with the remaining applications in that ballot.
190KJ First ballot
The first ballot of a calendar year must include—
its prescribed portion of the annual hectare limit; and
its reserved allocation of hectares for small applications; and
any hectares reallocated to the ballot in accordance with section 190KK(2).
Any hectares that are not included in the LUC class 6 permits that are issued following the first ballot may be reallocated as prescribed in regulations.
190KK Second ballot
In addition to any hectares referred to in section 190KJ(2), the second ballot must also include—
its prescribed portion of the annual hectare limit; and
its reserved allocation of hectares for small applications; and
any hectares reallocated to the ballot in accordance with section 190KL(1).
Any hectares that are not included in the LUC class 6 permits that are issued following the second ballot may be reallocated as prescribed in regulations,—
subject to the roll-over limit; and
if reallocated to a later year, subject to no further reallocation to any later calendar year.
190KL Reallocating unused small applications hectares
If the total number of hectares included in all of the eligible small applications that are drawn from the first ballot of a calendar year does not reach the reserved allocation for small applications in that ballot, the unused hectares must be reallocated into the second ballot of the year and made available as hectares that are not reserved for small applications.
If the total number of hectares included in all of the eligible small applications that are drawn from the second ballot of a calendar year does not reach the reserved allocation for small applications in that ballot, the unused hectares must be made available in that ballot as hectares that are not reserved for small applications.
When hectares are reallocated or made available under subsection (1) or (2), they do not affect or add to—
the allocation reserved in the second ballot for small applications; or
the prescribed portion of the annual hectare limit for that ballot.
 Ballot process
190KM Conditions of eligibility
To enter an application into a ballot for a LUC class 6 land permit, a person must—
specify the record or records of title comprising the land on which the LUC class 6 land to which the application relates is located; and
be the landowner of the land or provide the written consent of the landowner to make the application; and
make no more than 1 application in respect of the same record or records of title in that ballot; and
specify the number of hectares in respect of which the application is made, which must be no more than the area of LUC class 6 land contained in the record or records of title referred to in paragraph (a); and
ensure that the application—
is in the form, if any, prescribed by the EPA for that purpose; and
is accompanied by the prescribed fee, if any; and
provides any other prescribed information.
190KN Application ineligible if current permit includes record of title
An application for a LUC class 6 land permit is not eligible to be entered into a ballot if the application contains a record of title that is included in a LUC class 6 land permit that contains that record of title and is currently valid under section 190KY or 190KZ.
190KO Multiple applications with same record of title in common
If a record of title is included in more than 1 eligible application for a LUC class 6 land permit and one of those applications is drawn from a ballot, all remaining applications in the ballot that include that record of title become ineligible and are wholly cancelled.
190KP Retention of property-scale assessment records
An applicant must keep property-scale assessment records for 20 years if the applicant has used a property-scale assessment of the land use capability classification of land described in a record of title in an application for a LUC class 6 land permit.
190KQ Conducting ballot
The EPA must conduct a ballot by random selection—
in accordance with this Act and any regulations made under it; and
by using electro-mechanical drawing equipment or any other equipment that the EPA may determine.
190KR Review and appeal not available for decisions about entering application into ballot
There is no right of review or appeal under any of sections 144 to 146 in relation to a decision by the EPA as to whether or not to enter an application into a ballot for a LUC class 6 land permit.
 Issuing permits
190KS Effect of permit being issued
A person issued a LUC class 6 land permit may apply to be registered as a participant in relation to an activity of standard or permanent forestry in respect of LUC class 6 land or to add a carbon accounting area or areas to the post-1989 forest land in respect of which the person is recorded as a participant, as the case may be, up to the maximum number of hectares in respect of the LUC class 6 land permit.
The person may apply to register, or add, fewer hectares than the permit allows, but must not register or add more.
190KT EPA may decline application
The EPA may decline an application if the application does not meet the conditions of eligibility set out in section 190KM.
The EPA may decline an application partially in the circumstances set out in section 190KU.
190KU Exceeding remaining available hectares
If the final successful application in a ballot partially exceeds the remaining hectares available for that ballot, the EPA may issue a LUC class 6 land permit to the applicant for those remaining hectares but not the full number of hectares sought in the application.
 Use of LUC class 6 land permit
190KV Using LUC class 6 land permit
The holder of a LUC class 6 land permit must meet the requirements, conditions, and criteria under this Act in relation to applying for emissions trading scheme registration in respect of the LUC class 6 land, or adding that land to land that is already registered in the emissions trading scheme, as the case may be.
An application on the basis of a LUC class 6 land permit must also demonstrate to the EPA that the post-1989 forest land that the applicant seeks to register—
is contained within the record or records of title to which the LUC class 6 land permit relates; and
has an area equal to, or less than, the area of land in hectares that the LUC class 6 land permit provides.
190KW Permit not transferable to other person
Except as provided in this section, a permit is unusable if it is purported to be transferred to a person other than the person specified in the LUC class 6 land permit application.
If the person who is issued a LUC class 6 land permit dies while the permit is valid, the person to whom their estate in the LUC class 6 land is transmitted becomes the permit holder.
If an unincorporated body is issued a LUC class 6 land permit, the permit remains usable by the body while at least 60% of the members of the body are the same as when the permit was issued to the body.
In this section, a permit is usable if it may be used to register forest land in the emissions trading scheme.
190KX Permit not transferable to other land or when land subdivided
A LUC class 6 land permit cannot be used to register forest land in the emissions trading scheme other than land specified in the application for the LUC class 6 land permit.
If land described in a record or records of title to which a LUC class 6 land permit relates is subdivided, the permit is no longer valid.
 LUC class 6 land permit duration
190KY LUC class 6 land permit duration
The standard duration of a LUC class 6 land permit is that it is valid until the close of 31 December of the year that is 3 full years after the year in which the permit was issued.
However, a valid LUC class 6 land permit remains valid until a decision to approve or decline an ETS application to register or add land is made, if—
the permit is valid at the time the application is made; and
the application is full and complete; and
the application is made within the standard duration of a LUC class 6 land permit.
This section is subject to sections 190KZ, 190KZA, and 190KZB.
190KZ Extension of permit duration
The EPA may accept an ETS application using a LUC class 6 land permit until the close of 31 December of the year that is 6 full years after the year in which the permit is issued if the EPA is satisfied that a temporary adverse weather event or similar circumstances materially disrupted the applicant’s ability to register forest land before the expiry date.
190KZA Expiry of LUC class 6 land permit through use
When the EPA approves or declines either of the following that is made using a LUC class 6 land permit, the permit expires:
an application under section 57 to be registered as a participant:
an application under section 182C(5) to add a carbon accounting area to the post-1989 forest land in respect of which a person is recorded as a participant.
The unused or partially unused area of an expired permit is not—
able to be used by the holder under the expired permit; or
added to future ballots, unless regulations allow this.
190KZB Holder of LUC class 6 land permit may cancel permit
The holder of a LUC class 6 land permit may cancel the permit by notifying the EPA in the prescribed form.
The permit is cancelled on the date that the EPA receives the notification.
If the permit holder is not the landowner of the land to which the permit relates,—
the permit holder, when notifying the EPA, must provide the EPA with the landowner’s last known contact details; and
the EPA must take reasonable steps to notify the landowner, using the contact details provided in accordance with paragraph (a).
 EPA may report on ballots
190KZC EPA may report publicly about ballots
The EPA may report publicly on processes under this subpart, including information regarding receiving and approving applications and allocating the annual hectare limit.
 Review of annual hectare limit
190KZD Review of annual hectare limit
The Minister of Climate Change and the Minister of Agriculture, in consultation with the Minister of Forestry, may initiate a review of the annual hectare limit.
Reviews may take place in 2028, 2034, and at intervals of 5 years after 2034.
190KZE Regulations for changing annual hectare limit
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations to change the annual hectare limit.
The Minister may recommend the making of those regulations if that Minister and the Minister of Agriculture consider that the annual hectare limit should change, after they have undertaken consultation in accordance with sections 3A(b)﻿(xviab) and 3B(1)﻿(oab) and have considered the effect on the following matters of changing the limit:
meeting emissions budgets and national emission reduction targets:
the proper functioning of the emissions trading scheme:
the pace and scale of current and projected rural land use change to forestry:
economic considerations related to land use change:
any other matters the Ministers consider relevant.
Regulations made under this section must state which ballot the changed annual hectare limit will apply from.
Regulations made under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
 Subpart 5 — Averaging accounting methodology
191 Interpretation
In this subpart,—
carbon accounting area (averaging) has the meaning given in section 191B(2)
DCS (being the determined carbon stock for land) has the meaning given in section 191D(2)
first rotation forest has the meaning given in section 191C(3) and (4)
NACS (being the nominal average carbon stock for land) has the meaning given in section 191D(3)
subsequent rotation forest has the meaning given in section 191C(6)
TACS (being the typical average carbon stock for land) has the meaning given in section 191D(4).
191A Overview of averaging accounting methodology
The object of averaging accounting methodology is to account for emissions and removals from an activity of standard forestry—
by reference to the expected long-term average level of carbon stock of the land over multiple forest rotations, rather than by reference to short-term changes in the actual carbon stock of the land (as required by sections 63 and 64); and
in a way that achieves approximately the same result in the long term as would have been achieved using carbon stock change accounting but without the repeated receipt and surrender of units for each forest rotation.
The number of units that a participant for a carbon accounting area (averaging) is entitled to receive, or is liable to surrender,—
is determined by reference to the expected long-term average carbon stock of the land over multiple forest rotations and changes in that average (see sections 191E and 191F); and
will vary depending on whether the land has a first rotation forest or a subsequent rotation forest (see section 191C).
191B Averaging accounting applies to carbon accounting areas (averaging)
Averaging accounting methodology applies in respect of emissions and removals from an activity of standard forestry in a carbon accounting area (averaging).
A carbon accounting area in respect of which a participant is registered in respect of an activity of standard forestry is a carbon accounting area (averaging) if—
its constitution date is after 31 December 2022; and
it was constituted—
under section 182C from land that was not part of a previous carbon accounting area; or
under section 189B from land on which the initial activity was permanent forestry or PFSI activity; or
from a reconfiguration of 1 or more carbon accounting areas (averaging) (and no other land).
See also clause 33 of Schedule 1AA, which allows some other carbon accounting areas to be converted into carbon accounting areas (averaging).
191C First rotation forest and subsequent rotation forest
Land in a carbon accounting area (averaging) has either a first rotation forest or a subsequent rotation forest.
Different parts of a carbon accounting area (averaging) may have different rotation forests.
Land has a first rotation forest if—
the land has not been cleared since it became forest land; or
the land,—
having been forest land, was deforested; and
remained deforested for at least the stand-down period prescribed in regulations made under section 191I (but see subsection (5)); and
was re-established as forest land; and
has not been cleared since that re-establishment; or
the land—
is post-1989 forest land because of paragraph (a)﻿(iii) to (vii) of the definition of post-1989 forest land in section 4; and
has not been cleared since it became post-1989 forest land; or
the land is declared by regulations made under section 191I to have a first rotation forest.
However, land that would otherwise have a first rotation forest under subsection (3) does not have a first rotation forest if it is declared by regulations made under section 191I to have a subsequent rotation forest.
Subsection (3)﻿(b)﻿(ii) does not apply if the deforestation referred to in subsection (3)﻿(b)﻿(i) occurred before 1 January 2021.
Land has a subsequent rotation forest if it does not have a first rotation forest.
191D Carbon stock measures for land
There are 3 measures of carbon stock for land in a carbon accounting area (averaging)—
the determined carbon stock:
the nominal average carbon stock:
the typical average carbon stock.
The determined carbon stock (DCS) of land is the current level of carbon stock of the land determined in accordance with the prescribed methodology.
The nominal average carbon stock (NACS) for land is the average carbon stock of the land if the length of each forest rotation is assumed to be equal to,—
if the land has a first rotation forest, the current age of the forest species on the land:
if the land has a subsequent rotation forest, the prior clearing age,
determined in accordance with the prescribed methodology.
The typical average carbon stock (TACS) for land is the average carbon stock of the land if the average length of each forest rotation is assumed to be equal to,—
if the land has a first rotation forest, the average expected rotation length for land of that kind:
if the land has a subsequent rotation forest, the prior clearing age,
determined in accordance with the prescribed methodology.
In this section,—
average carbon stock, of land, means the expected long-term average level of carbon stock of the land over multiple forest rotations
prescribed methodology means the methodology prescribed in regulations made under section 191I
prior clearing age, for land that has a subsequent rotation forest, means the age of the forest species that were cleared from the land at the end of the previous rotation at the time they were cleared, determined in accordance with the prescribed methodology.
191E Entitlement to receive units
The participant in respect of a carbon accounting area (averaging) is entitled to receive New Zealand units for removals from the carbon accounting area in accordance with regulations made under section 191I.
In general terms, the participant will be entitled to receive units as follows:
in the case of land that has a first rotation forest,—
for removals that occur while the land’s DCS is less than its TACS; and
if—
the land’s DCS is equal to or greater than its TACS; and
the land’s NACS increases to an amount that is greater than its TACS:
in the case of land that has a second rotation forest, if its NACS increases to an amount that is greater than its TACS.
If the regulations prescribe different NACS or TACS (or both) for different classes of land, the participant may also be entitled to receive units if the land changes from one class to another with a different NACS or TACS (or both).
191F Liability to surrender units
The participant in respect of a carbon accounting area (averaging) is liable to surrender units for emissions from the carbon accounting area in accordance with regulations made under section 191I.
In general terms, the participant is liable to surrender units as follows:
in the case of land that has a first rotation forest, for emissions that occur when—
the land’s DCS is greater than its NACS; and
the land’s NACS is less than its TACS:
in the case of land that has a subsequent rotation forest, if the land’s NACS decreases to an amount that is less than its TACS.
If the regulations prescribe different NACS or TACS (or both) for different classes of land, the participant may also be liable to surrender units if the land changes from one class to another with a different NACS or TACS (or both).
The participant is also liable to surrender units if the land is deforested.
191G Calculating and reporting emissions and removals
If provided in the regulations, the participant in respect of a carbon accounting area (averaging) is not required to—
calculate emissions and removals for which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units; or
submit emissions returns covering a carbon accounting area (averaging) in relation to which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units.
191H Restriction on reconfiguration
Carbon accounting areas cannot be reconfigured (whether by application under section 188 or by any other process that requires the submission of a new unit balance report) so as to combine in a CAA2 land from a CAA1 that is a carbon accounting area (averaging) and land from a CAA1 that is not.
191I Regulations for averaging
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
prescribing—
how emissions and removals from an activity of standard forestry in a carbon accounting area (averaging) must be calculated and reported:
the circumstances in which a participant is or is not liable to surrender units, or entitled to receive New Zealand units, for those emissions and removals:
the methodology for determining the number of units the participant is entitled to receive or liable to surrender in those circumstances:
providing that a participant for a carbon accounting area (averaging) is not required to—
calculate emissions and removals for which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units:
submit emissions returns for a carbon accounting area (averaging) in relation to which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units:
for the purposes of the definition of first rotation forest (see section 191C),—
prescribing the stand-down period:
declaring land to have a first rotation forest or a subsequent rotation forest:
prescribing methodologies for determining DCS, NACS, TACS, and prior clearing age (see section 191D):
providing for any other matters contemplated by this subpart, necessary for its administration, or necessary for giving it full effect.
Regulations made under this section may make different provision for different cases on any differential basis, including—
for different forest species:
for forest species of different ages:
for different rotation periods:
for different parts of New Zealand.
Regulations made under this section may have retrospective effect as follows:
regulations may apply on and from the commencement of the mandatory emissions return period in which they are made or a later date in that period:
regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(c)﻿(i) may prescribe a stand-down period that begins before the regulations are made.
However, regulations cannot be made under subsection (1)﻿(c)﻿(i) that would increase the length of the stand-down period for a carbon accounting area whose constitution date is before those regulations come into force.
Regulations made under this section may require the use of a computer programme available via the Internet site of the EPA.
Regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(b) may relate to emissions or removals that—
stem directly from an activity; or
are associated with a product or other thing that is the subject of an activity.
See sections 3A and 3B for consultation requirements that apply to the making of regulations under this section.
See also sections 169 to 175 (incorporation by reference).
Regulations made under this section come into force 3 months after the date of their publication under the Legislation Act 2019, or on any later date specified in the regulations.
Regulations under this section—
are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements); and
have effect in accordance with subsection (3) even if they are not yet published.
 Subpart 6 — P89 offsetting
192 Interpretation
In this subpart,—
expected carbon stock, for P89 offsetting (forested) land, has the meaning given in section 192I
P89 offset application means an application to the EPA submitted under section 192A
P89 offset application date means the date on which a P89 offset application is submitted to the EPA under section 192A
P89 offset date, for P89 offsetting (approved) land, has the meaning given in section 192C(2)﻿(g)
P89 offset release criteria has the meaning given in section 192I
P89 offsetting (approved) land means land that—
has become P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192C(2)﻿(f) or 192S(2)﻿(c); and
has not ceased to be P89 offsetting (approved) land under a provision referred to in section 192D
P89 offsetting (excess) land means land that—
has become P89 offsetting (excess) land under section 192J(2)﻿(a)﻿(iii); and
has not ceased to be P89 offsetting (excess) land under section 192N(3)
P89 offsetting (forested) land has the meaning given in section 192J
P89 offsetting (unforested) land has the meaning given in section 192J
P89 release criteria notice means a notice given under section 192J(1)
qualifying forest land has the meaning given in section 192J(3)
reference carbon stock, for a CAA1, has the meaning given in section 192A(2)﻿(e).
 P89 offset applications
192A Application to offset land for land in carbon accounting area (averaging)
A participant in an activity of standard forestry on 1 or more carbon accounting areas (averaging) may apply to the EPA to offset other land for those areas (to transfer the unit balance from the carbon accounting areas (averaging) to the new land).
The application must—
specify the carbon accounting areas (averaging) to which the application relates (each a CAA1); and
specify the land proposed to be used to offset each CAA1; and
include a final forestry emissions return prepared under section 183B for the activity—
that covers the CAA1s; and
that uses the P89 offset application date as the relevant date; and
include in that return a new unit balance report prepared under section 185 that covers 1 or more carbon accounting areas (each a CAA2) for each CAA1 consisting of the land specified under paragraph (b); and
include—
the carbon stock of each CAA1 on the P89 offset application date determined in accordance with regulations made under section 192U (the reference carbon stock for the CAA1); and
if the person proposed as the participant in respect of any of the CAA2s is not already registered as a participant, the information necessary for that person to become registered; and
any other information prescribed in regulations made under section 192U.
If the person proposed as the participant in respect of any of the CAA2s is not the participant in respect of the CAA1s, the application must be made jointly with that other person.
The application must—
be signed by all of the applicants; and
be submitted—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
192B Criteria for P89 offset application
If a person submits a P89 offset application, the EPA,—
if satisfied that the criteria in subsection (2) were met on the P89 offset application date, must approve the application; or
otherwise, may decline the application.
The criteria are that—
the land in each CAA1 is 1 or both of the following:
land that has a first rotation forest and whose NACS is equal to or greater than its TACS (as those terms are defined in section 191D):
land that has a subsequent rotation forest; and
the predominant forest species on each CAA1 were established by direct planting activities, including direct seeding but excluding natural forest regeneration; and
the land in each CAA2 is 1 or more of the following:
land that is not forest land on the P89 offset application date, but if it were to become forest land—
would be post-1989 forest land; and
if it were in a carbon accounting area, would meet the criteria in section 191C for having a first rotation forest:
post-1989 forest land that—
became post-1989 forest land less than 2 years before the P89 offset application date; and
meets the criteria in section 191C for having a first rotation forest (or would do so if it were in a carbon accounting area):
area 2 (excess) land (as defined in section 181), unless the re-use period prescribed in regulations made under section 192U has expired:
P89 offsetting (excess) land, unless the re-use period prescribed in regulations made under section 192U has expired; and
the total area (whether or not contiguous) of the CAA2s for a CAA1 is equal to or greater than the area of that CAA1; and
each individual parcel that makes up the CAA2 has an area of at least 1 hectare and has an average width of at least 30 metres; and
the participant in respect of each CAA2 would, if the land in the CAA2 were forest land,—
if they are not already registered as a participant in the activity, qualify to be registered under section 57; and
qualify under sections 182A and 182AB to be registered as a participant in respect of the CAA2; and
the EPA is satisfied that, on the P89 offset date, the P89 offset release criteria are likely to be met in respect of each CAA1 and the CAA2s proposed in respect of it; and
any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 192U are met.
192C Effect of approval of P89 offset application
This section applies if the EPA approves a P89 offset application.
Starting on the P89 offset application date,—
the emissions return for the CAA1s is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA1s under section 184D); and
if any of the land proposed to be included in a CAA2 is already in a carbon accounting area, the participant for that land—
is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the unit balance of that carbon accounting area; and
ceases to be a participant in the relevant activity in that carbon accounting area; and
the persons proposed as participants for CAA2s are participants in the activity in the CAA2s; and
the participant in respect of the CAA1s—
ceases to be a participant in the activity in the CAA1s; and
is not liable to surrender the unit balances of the CAA1s; and
the unit balance of each CAA2 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report; and
the land in the CAA2s for a CAA1 is the P89 offsetting (approved) land for that CAA1; and
the P89 offset date for the P89 offsetting (approved) land for a CAA1 is,—
if, on the P89 offset application date, every hectare of land in the CAA1 had forest species on it that had tree crown cover of more than 30%, 4 years after the P89 offset application date; or
if not, 4 years after the clearing of the CAA1 started most recently before the P89 offset application date.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
If subsection (2)﻿(b)﻿(i) applies,—
the notice requiring those units to be surrendered (referred to in section 63(4)) must also set out the effect of paragraph (b); and
if the units are not surrendered by the due date, the EPA may revoke the approval by giving written notice to the participant.
If an approval is revoked under subsection (4), this Act applies as if the application had never been made.
However, despite subsection (5), the effect of subsection (2)﻿(b)﻿(ii) is not reversed (so the person is not reinstated as a participant for the carbon accounting area).
 P89 offsetting (approved) land
192D Duration of P89 offsetting (approved) land status
Land that becomes P89 offsetting (approved) land for a CAA1 under section 192C(2)﻿(f) remains P89 offsetting (approved) land until one of the following occurs:
the land meets the P89 offset release criteria and is released from being P89 offsetting (approved) land on the P89 offset date under section 192N(2)﻿(c):
the person ceases to be a participant because of section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest):
the land becomes temporary adverse event land under section 193C:
the land is P89 offsetting (unforested) land and ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192N(2)﻿(d):
the land is P89 offsetting (excess) land and ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192N(2)﻿(e):
the land is declared to not be P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192O(3) and ceases to be so under section 192P(2)﻿(a):
the land is removed land and ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192S(2)﻿(d).
To avoid doubt, the land continues to be P89 offsetting (approved) land even if the carbon accounting areas containing the land are reconfigured (whether under section 188B or by any other process that requires the submission of a new unit balance report).
192E Effect of being P89 offsetting (approved) land
All of the provisions of this Act that apply to post-1989 forest land that is in a carbon accounting area (averaging) apply to P89 offsetting (approved) land as if it were post-1989 forest land, subject to sections 192F and 192G.
192F Subsequent rotation forest
P89 offsetting (approved) land is to be treated as having a subsequent rotation forest (despite section 191C(3)).
Subsection (1) continues to apply to P89 offsetting (forested) land (identified under section 192J) until—
it is first cleared after the P89 offset date (even though it ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land on the P89 offset date); or
the participant becomes registered for an activity of permanent forestry on the land.
To avoid doubt, when subsection (1) ceases to apply to any land, section 191C applies.
192G Reconfiguration restrictions
A carbon accounting area containing P89 offsetting (approved) land cannot be reconfigured (whether by application under section 188 or by any other process that requires the submission of a new unit balance report) except as permitted by subsection (2).
Reconfiguration is permitted—
to reconfigure the carbon accounting areas that contain the P89 offsetting (approved) land for the same CAA1 without including any other land:
to remove land that is affected by a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest in accordance with sections 182G and 186B:
to remove land that becomes temporary adverse event land under section 193C:
on the P89 offset date as required under section 192J:
to substitute land under sections 192Q to 192S.
192H No transfers to permanent forestry
A participant for a carbon accounting area containing P89 offsetting (approved) land cannot apply under section 189 to become a participant in an activity of permanent forestry on that land.
 Offsetting on P89 offset date
192I P89 offset release criteria
The P89 offset release criteria in respect of a CAA1 and its P89 offsetting (approved) land that is P89 offsetting (forested) land are that, on the P89 offset date,—
the area of the P89 offsetting (forested) land is equal to or greater than the area of CAA1; and
the expected carbon stock of the P89 offsetting (forested) land is equal to or greater than the reference carbon stock of the CAA1; and
any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 192U are met.
The expected carbon stock of land is the carbon stock (determined in accordance with regulations made under section 192U) that the land is expected to have achieved at the end of the period prescribed in regulations made under that section.
If any of the P89 offsetting (approved) land ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192D(1)﻿(b) or (c) (because of adverse events) on or before the P89 offset date,—
for subsection (1)﻿(a), the area of the CAA1 on the P89 offset application date is to be treated as reduced in accordance with subsection (4); and
for subsection (1)﻿(b), the reference carbon stock for the CAA1 is to be treated as reduced in accordance with subsection (5).
The reduced area of the CAA1 (in hectares) (y) is calculated as follows:
where—
is the area of the CAA1 on the P89 offset application date (in hectares)
is the area of the land that ceased to be P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192D(1)﻿(b) or (c) (in hectares)
is the area of the P89 offsetting (approved) land on the P89 offset application date (being all of the CAA2s under section 192A) (in hectares).
The reduced reference carbon stock for the CAA1 (in tonnes) (w) is calculated as follows:
where—
is the reference carbon stock for the CAA1 (in tonnes)
is the area of the land that ceased to be P89 offsetting (approved) land under section 192D(1)﻿(b) or (c) (in hectares)
is the area of the P89 offsetting (approved) land on the P89 offset application date (being all of the CAA2s under section 192A) (in hectares).
192J P89 release criteria notice
The participants in an activity of standard forestry on the P89 offsetting (approved) land for a CAA1 on the P89 offset date must give notice (P89 release criteria notice) to the EPA of the extent of compliance with the P89 offset release criteria on the P89 offset date.
The P89 release criteria notice must—
identify all of the P89 offsetting (approved) land that is each of the following:
P89 offsetting (forested) land, being all the P89 offsetting (approved) land that is qualifying forest land on the P89 offset date, other than P89 offsetting (excess) land:
P89 offsetting (unforested) land, being all the P89 offsetting (approved) land that is not qualifying forest land on the P89 offset date:
P89 offsetting (excess) land, being any P89 offsetting (approved) land that—
is qualifying forest land on the P89 offset date; and
does not need to be part of the P89 offsetting (forested) land in order for the P89 offset release criteria to be met; and
the participants want to be excluded from the P89 offsetting (forested) land and to be available for re-use under section 181B(2)﻿(b)﻿(iv) or 192B(2)﻿(c)﻿(iv); and
include final forestry emissions returns under section 183B for each participant and activity—
that covers each carbon accounting area that contains the P89 offsetting (approved) land (each a CAA3); and
that uses the P89 offset date as the relevant date; and
include in each return a P89 offset date unit balance report under section 192M that covers the following carbon accounting areas (each a CAA4) formed from each CAA3:
1 or more forested CAA4s for the P89 offsetting (forested) land in the CAA3:
an unforested CAA4 for any P89 offsetting (unforested) land in the CAA3:
1 or more excess CAA4s for any P89 offsetting (excess) land in the CAA3; and
include any information prescribed in regulations made under section 192U.
Land is qualifying forest land if—
each hectare of land has forest species on it that have, or are likely to have, tree crown cover of more than 30%; and
those forest species were established by direct planting activities, including direct seeding but excluding natural forest regeneration; and
each individual parcel that makes up the land has an area of at least 1 hectare and has an average width of at least 30 metres; and
the land has not been declared not to be qualifying forest land under section 192O(2).
The P89 release criteria notice must—
be made jointly by the participants in respect of all of the P89 offsetting (approved) land for the CAA1; and
be signed by all of the participants; and
be given within 60 working days after the P89 offset date; and
be given—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
In relation to a final forestry emissions return required by subsection (2)﻿(b), sections 183B to 184D apply as if the references in those sections to CAA1 were references to CAA3.
Sections 183B to 185, and the provisions applied by them, apply as if the land in an unforested CAA4 were forest land.
192K Liability to surrender units if P89 offset release criteria not met
This section applies in relation to the CAA3s for a CAA1 if the P89 offset release criteria under either or both of paragraphs (a) and (b) of section 192I(1) are not met.
The participants for the CAA3 are liable to surrender the number of units determined under subsections (4) to (6).
That liability is apportioned between the CAA3s under section 192L.
If the criterion in section 192I(1)﻿(a) is not met, the number of units to be surrendered (sa) is calculated as follows:

192L Maximum liability and apportionment
This section applies if the participants for the CAA3s for a CAA1 have a liability under section 192K.
If there is only one CAA3,—
section 185A applies; and
the liability for that CAA3 is equal to t under section 192K(6).
If there are 2 or more CAA3s,—
section 185A does not apply; but
if the total liability calculated under section 192K(6) (t) is greater than the total of the unit balances of all of the CAA3s, t is reduced to be equal to that total unit balance.

192M P89 offset date unit balance report
A P89 offset date unit balance report required by section 192J(2)﻿(c) must—
specify the CAA4s that the report covers and, for each CAA4 whose boundaries are not the same as a CAA3, define the CAA4; and
specify the CAA3s (whose land will form the CAA4s); and
specify the opening unit balance of each unforested CAA4 and each excess CAA4 (if any) as zero; and
set out the calculation under subsection (2) of the opening unit balance of each forested CAA4.
The opening unit balance of a forested CAA4 (v) is calculated as follows:
