is the area of the forested CAA4 (in hectares)
is the total area of all of the P89 offsetting (forested) land for the CAA1 (in hectares).
192N Effect on P89 offset date
This section applies if a P89 release criteria notice is given to the EPA in accordance with section 192J, including a final forestry emissions return (for the CAA3s) and a P89 offset date unit balance report (for the CAA4s).
Starting on the P89 offset date,—
the emissions return for the CAA3s is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA3s under section 184D); and
the participants are not liable to surrender the unit balances of each CAA3 (although they may be liable to surrender units under section 192K); and
for each forested CAA4,—
the person who was the participant in respect of the CAA3 from which it was formed is the participant in respect of the forested CAA4 (instead of the CAA3); and
the land in the forested CAA4 is released from being P89 offsetting (approved) land; and
the unit balance of each forested CAA4 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the P89 offset date unit balance report; and
for each unforested CAA4,—
the person who was the participant in respect of the CAA3 from which it was formed ceases to be a participant in respect of the unforested CAA4; and
the land in the unforested CAA4 ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land; and
the unit balance of each unforested CAA4 is zero; and
for each excess CAA4,—
the person who was the participant in respect of the CAA3 from which it was formed is the participant in respect of the excess CAA4; and
the land in the excess CAA4 ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land but remains P89 offsetting (excess) land subject to subsection (3)); and
the unit balance of each excess CAA4 is zero.
Land ceases to be P89 offsetting (excess) land if—
the land becomes area 2 (approved) land as a result of being included in a P90 offset application under section 181B(2)﻿(b)﻿(iii); or
the land becomes P89 offsetting (approved) land as a result of being included in a P89 offset application under section 192B(2)﻿(c)﻿(iii); or
all re-use periods prescribed in regulations made under section 181W or 192U expire.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
After the P89 offset date, section 179 (except subsection (1)﻿(a)) applies to the P89 offsetting (forested) land as if it had been cleared on the P89 offset application date.
Subsection (5) ceases to apply when that land is next cleared (after which section 179 will apply).
 Action if criteria for initial P89 offset application not met
192O EPA may take action if original criteria not met
This section applies if the EPA—
approved a P89 offset application; but
is now satisfied that the application should not have been approved because some or all of the P89 offsetting (approved) land did not meet the criteria in section 192B(2)﻿(c) or (e) or any applicable criteria prescribed for section 192B(2)﻿(h).
If this section applies to land on or before the P89 offset date, the EPA may declare that the land that did not meet those criteria is not qualifying forest land for the purposes of section 192J.
If this section applies to land after the P89 offset date, the EPA may declare that section 192P applies to the carbon accounting area that now contains the land that did not meet those criteria.
The EPA cannot make a declaration under this section more than 7 years after the P89 offset application date.
Before making a declaration, the EPA must—
notify the participant of its intention to do so and the grounds for doing so; and
give the participant at least 60 working days to—
show cause as to why the EPA should not do so; or
take other remedial action specified in the notice (see section 192Q).
If the EPA makes a declaration under this section, it must give the participant notice of—
the declaration and the date on which it was made; and
the participant’s liability under section 192P (if applicable); and
the participant’s right under section 144 to seek a review of the decision to make the declaration.
192P Effect of declaration after P89 offset date
This section applies if the EPA makes a declaration under section 192O(3) that this section applies to a carbon accounting area (the CAA).
Starting on the date on which the declaration is made, the participant in respect of the CAA—
is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the unit balance of the CAA; and
ceases to be a participant in respect of the CAA.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
192Q Remedial action: land substitution
In a notice under section 192O(5), the EPA may give the participant the option to take remedial action by substituting other land for land that did not meet the criteria (the non-compliant land).
A participant given that option may apply to the EPA to do so.
The application must—
specify the carbon accounting areas (each a CAA5) that include the non-compliant land for which other land is to be substituted; and
identify all the land in each CAA5 as either—
removed land, being the non-compliant land for which other land is to be substituted; or
remaining land, being all the land in the CAA5 that is not removed land; and
identify the land that is proposed to be substituted for the removed land (substitute land); and
include a final forestry emissions return prepared under section 183B for the relevant activity—
that covers the CAA5s; and
that uses the date on which the application is submitted to the EPA as the relevant date; and
include in that return a new unit balance report that—
relates to the following carbon accounting areas (each a CAA6):
a removed CAA6 for the removed land:
a remaining CAA6 for the remaining land:
a substitute CAA6 for the substitute land; and
specifies the opening unit balance of the removed CAA6 as zero; and
calculates the opening unit balance for the remaining CAA6s and substitute CAA6s in accordance with section 185(4), which applies even though a CAA6 is formed from land in 1 or more CAA5s and from other land; and
is otherwise prepared under section 185; and
include any information prescribed in regulations made under section 192U.
The application must—
be signed by the participant; and
be given within the period specified in the notice under section 192O(5); and
be given—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
In relation to the final forestry emissions return and new unit balance report required by subsection (3)﻿(d) and (e), sections 183B to 185 apply as if—
the references in those sections to CAA1 were references to CAA5; and
the references in those sections to CAA2 were references to CAA6.
Sections 183B to 185, and the provisions applied by them, apply as if the land in a removed CAA6 were forest land.
192R Criteria for land substitution
If a person submits an application under section 192Q to substitute land, the EPA,—
if satisfied that the criteria in subsection (2) are met, must approve the application; or
otherwise, may decline the application.
The criteria are that—
the substitute land is land of a kind specified in 1 or more of subparagraphs (i) to (iv) of section 192B(2)﻿(c); and
the area of the substitute land is equal to or greater than the area of the removed land; and
the EPA is satisfied that,—
if the substitution date is before the P89 offset date, the P89 offset release criteria are likely to be met in respect of the CAA1 and the new P89 offsetting (approved) land; or
if the substitution date is on or after the P89 offset date, the expected carbon stock of the new P89 offsetting (approved) land as at the substitution date was equal to or greater than the reference carbon stock of the CAA1; and
any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 192U are met.
In this section,—
new P89 offsetting (approved) land means all of the land that will be P89 offsetting (approved) land for the CAA1 if the application is approved
substitution date means the date on which the application under section 192Q was submitted.
192S Effect of approval of land substitution
This section applies if the EPA approves an application under section 192R.
Starting on the date on which the application was submitted,—
the emissions return for the CAA5s is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA5s under section 184D); and
if any of the land in a substitute CAA6 is already in a carbon accounting area, the participant for that land—
is liable to surrender the number of units equal to the unit balance of that carbon accounting area; and
ceases to be a participant in the relevant activity on that carbon accounting area; and
in respect of the remainder CAA6s and substitute CAA6s,—
the person becomes a participant in the activity in those CAA6s (instead of the CAA5s); and
the unit balance of each of those CAA6s is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report; and
the land in those CAA6s is P89 offsetting (approved) land for the original CAA1 (together with any P89 offsetting (approved) land for the CAA1 that was not included in this application); and
in respect of each removed CAA6,—
the person ceases to be a participant in the activity of the removed CAA6; and
the land ceases to be P89 offsetting (approved) land; and
the person is not liable to surrender units (because the unit balance is zero).
If subsection (2)﻿(b)﻿(i) applies,—
the notice requiring those units to be surrendered (referred to in section 63(4)) must also set out the effect of paragraph (b); and
if the units are not surrendered by the due date, the EPA may revoke the approval by giving written notice to the participant.
If an approval is revoked under subsection (3), this Act applies as if the application had never been made.
However, despite subsection (4), the effect of subsection (2)﻿(b)﻿(ii) is not reversed (so the person is not reinstated as a participant for the carbon accounting area).
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
To avoid doubt, the substitution of land under this section does not affect the P89 offset date for the CAA1.
192T Effect of application to substitute land being declined
This section applies if the EPA declines an application under section 192R.
The participant cannot make another application under section 192Q.
The EPA—
may make the declaration under section 192O; and
if it does so, the notice under section 192O(6) must also notify the participants of—
the decision to decline the application under section 192R, and the reasons for it; and
the participants’ right under section 144 to seek a review of that decision.
 Regulations
192U Regulations for P89 offsetting
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
prescribing the methodology for determining—
reference carbon stock (section 192A):
expected carbon stock (section 192I(2)):
prescribing the information to be included in, and other requirements for,—
P89 offset applications (section 192A):
P89 release criteria notices (section 192J):
applications to substitute land (section 192Q):
prescribing re-use periods for area 2 (excess) land or P89 offsetting (excess) land (section 192B(2)﻿(c)﻿(iii) and (iv)):
prescribing additional criteria for the approval of—
P89 offset applications (section 192B(2)﻿(h)):
land substitution applications (section 192R(2)﻿(d)):
prescribing additional P89 offset release criteria (section 192I(1)﻿(c)):
prescribing the period for the purposes of the definition of expected carbon stock (section 192I(2)):
providing for any other matters contemplated by this subpart, necessary for its administration, or necessary for giving it full effect.
Regulations made under this section may make different provision for different cases on any differential basis, including—
for different forest species:
for forest species of different ages:
for different rotation periods:
for different parts of New Zealand.
Regulations made under this section may have retrospective effect to the extent that they may apply on and from the commencement of the mandatory emissions return period in which they are made or a later date in that period.
Regulations made under this section may require the use of a computer programme available via the Internet site of the EPA.
See sections 3A and 3B for consultation requirements that apply to the making of regulations under this section.
See also sections 169 to 175 (incorporation by reference).
Regulations under this section—
are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements); and
have effect in accordance with subsection (3) even if they are not yet published.
 Subpart 7 — Temporary adverse events
193 Interpretation for this subpart
In this subpart,—
adverse event, in relation to temporary adverse event land, means the event referred to in section 193A(1)﻿(b) as a result of which the land became temporary adverse event land
affected land has the meaning given in section 193A(1)
carbon recovery has the meaning given in section 193M
event date, in relation to an adverse event, means the later of,—
if the event occurs—
on only 1 day, that day; or
over 2 or more days, the first of those days (even if land in a particular carbon accounting area is not affected until the second or a later day of the event); and
if the event occurs in circumstances specified in regulations made under section 193R, the date provided for in the regulations
non-established land has the meaning given in section 193K
permanently affected land has the meaning given in section 193K
pre-event carbon stock rate, for temporary adverse event land from a CAA1, means the average carbon stock per hectare of the affected land in the CAA1 on the day before the event date, determined in accordance with regulations made under section 193R, unless subsection (2) applies
re-established land has the meaning given in section 193K
re-establishment has the meaning given in section 193J
re-establishment date has the meaning given in section 193J
temporary adverse event land means land that has become temporary adverse event land under section 193C(2)﻿(e) and has not ceased to be so under a provision referred to in section 193D.
For the purposes of the definition of pre-event carbon stock rate,—
if land that is temporary adverse event land in relation to an adverse event (event 1) becomes temporary adverse event land in relation to a later adverse event (event 2), the pre-event carbon stock rate for the land in relation to event 2 is the same as the pre-event carbon stock rate it had in relation to event 1; and
if P89 offsetting (approved) land becomes temporary adverse event land, the pre-event carbon stock rate for the land is the reference carbon stock (under section 192A) per hectare of the CAA1 for which the land is P89 offsetting (approved) land.
 Application
193A Application for temporary adverse event suspension
Post-1989 forest land is affected land if—
the land is in a carbon accounting area (a CAA1); and
the land is affected by an event of a kind prescribed in regulations made under section 193R (the adverse event); and
the event results in each hectare of land ceasing to have forest species on it that have, or are likely to have, tree crown cover of more than 30%; and
the area of land in each CAA1 that is affected by the event is equal to or greater than any minimum prescribed in regulations made under section 193R; and
the extent of carbon stock lost from the land affected by the event is equal to or greater than any minimum prescribed in regulations made under section 193R.
A participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in a CAA1 may apply for a temporary adverse event suspension for the affected land in that CAA1.
The application must—
specify the CAA1s to which the application relates; and
include a final forestry emissions return prepared under section 183B for the activity—
that covers the CAA1s; and
that uses the day before the event date as the relevant date; and
include in that return a new unit balance report prepared under section 185 for the activity that covers the following carbon accounting areas (CAA2s) formed from each CAA1:
an affected CAA2 for the affected land in the CAA1:
a remainder CAA2 for the rest of the land in the CAA1; and
include—
the pre-event carbon stock rate for the affected land; and
any other information prescribed in regulations made under section 193R.
The application must—
be signed by the participant; and
be submitted by the deadline prescribed in regulations made under section 193R; and
be submitted—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
193B Criteria of temporary adverse event suspension
If a person submits an application under section 193A for a temporary adverse event suspension, the EPA,—
if satisfied that the criteria in subsection (2) are met, must approve the application; or
otherwise, may decline the application.
The criteria are that—
the land in the affected CAA2s is affected land; and
the participant notified the EPA of the occurrence of the adverse event in accordance with regulations made under section 193R; and
the EPA is satisfied that the land in the affected CAA2s—
is likely to achieve re-establishment under section 193J; and
is likely to achieve carbon recovery under section 193M; and
any other criteria prescribed in regulations made under section 193R are met.
193C Approval of temporary adverse event suspension
This section applies if the EPA approves an application for a temporary adverse event suspension under section 193B.
Starting on the day before the event date,—
the emissions return for the CAA1s is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA1s under section 184D); and
the person is a participant in the activity on the CAA2s (instead of the CAA1s); and
the person is not liable to surrender the unit balance of each CAA1; and
the unit balance of each CAA2 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report; and
the land in the affected CAA2s formed from a CAA1 is the temporary adverse event land from that CAA1 in relation to the adverse event.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
 Temporary adverse event land
193D Duration of temporary adverse event land status
Land that becomes temporary adverse event land under section 193C(2)﻿(e) remains temporary adverse event land until one of the following occurs:
the land is released from being temporary adverse event land under section 193N (having achieved carbon recovery and passed the re-establishment date):
the person ceases to be a participant because of section 182G (for a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest), whether as a result of the adverse event or a different event:
the land is affected by another event and becomes temporary adverse event land under section 193C(2)﻿(e) in relation to that later event:
the land is non-established land and ceases to be temporary adverse event land under section 193L(2)﻿(d):
the land is permanently affected land and ceases to be temporary adverse event land under section 193L(2)﻿(e):
the land ceases to be temporary adverse event land under section 193O because of a breach of condition:
the land ceases to be temporary adverse event land under section 193P because it is intentionally converted, cleared before it achieves carbon recovery, or is to be treated as deforested.
To avoid doubt, the land continues to be temporary adverse event land even if the carbon accounting areas containing the land—
are reconfigured (whether under section 188B or by any other process that requires the submission of a new unit balance report); or
change activity from standard forestry to permanent forestry or vice versa.
193E Effect of being temporary adverse event land
All of the provisions of this Act that apply to post-1989 forest land continue to apply to temporary adverse event land as if it remained forest land, subject to sections 193F to 193I.
The temporary adverse event land is also subject to any conditions prescribed in regulations made under section 193R.
193F No liability or entitlement
A participant in respect of temporary adverse event land is not liable to surrender units, or entitled to receive New Zealand units, for emissions and removals for the land (including emissions resulting from the adverse event).
However, subsection (1) is subject to sections 189F and 189G, and the participant is liable to surrender units under those sections if they apply.
If provided in regulations made under section 193R, the participant is not required to—
calculate emissions and removals for which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units; or
submit emissions returns covering a carbon accounting area in relation to which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units.
193G First rotation forest
If temporary adverse event land is in a carbon accounting area (averaging), and on the day before the event date the land had a first rotation forest, then the land is to be treated as continuing to have a first rotation forest.
Subsection (1) continues to apply in relation to re-established land until it is first cleared after the re-establishment date (even though it ceases to be temporary adverse event land on the re-establishment date).
To avoid doubt, when subsection (1) ceases to apply to land, section 191C applies.
193H Reconfiguration restrictions
A carbon accounting area containing temporary adverse event land cannot be reconfigured (whether by application under section 188 or by any other process that requires the submission of a new unit balance report) except as permitted by subsection (2).
Reconfiguration is permitted—
to reconfigure the carbon accounting areas that contain the temporary adverse event land from the same CAA1 without including any other land:
to remove land that is affected by a natural event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest in accordance with sections 182G and 186B (whether that is the adverse event or a different event):
to remove land that becomes temporary adverse event land in relation to a different event:
on the re-establishment date as required under section 193K:
to remove land that has ceased to be temporary adverse event land when section 193Q(5) applies.
193I Damage to land turns out to be permanent
If the adverse event was a natural event and it becomes apparent that it permanently prevents the re-establishing of a forest on the land,—
if that becomes apparent before the re-establishment date, the participant may notify the EPA under section 182G (then see section 193D(1)﻿(b)); or
if that is apparent at the re-establishment date and the participant has not notified the EPA under section 182G, the participant must identify the land as permanently affected land under section 193K (then see section 193L(2)﻿(e)); or
if that becomes apparent after the re-establishment date, the participant may notify the EPA under section 182G (then see section 193D(1)﻿(b)).
To avoid doubt, if P89 offsetting (approved) land is affected by another event that permanently prevents re-establishing a forest on that land, the participant may comply with section 182G in relation to that event.
 Re-establishment
193J Re-establishment criteria
A hectare of temporary adverse event land achieves re-establishment if, on the re-establishment date, the hectare has forest species on it that have, or are likely to have, tree crown cover of more than 30%.
The re-establishment date, in relation to an adverse event, means the later of—
the date 4 years after the event date; and
in circumstances specified in regulations made under section 193R, the date provided for in the regulations.
193K Notice of achievement of re-establishment
A participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in 1 or more carbon accounting areas that contain temporary adverse event land from a CAA1 (each a CAA3) must give notice to the EPA of the extent to which the temporary adverse event land has achieved re-establishment.
The notice must,—
for each CAA3, identify all of the land in the CAA3 that is each of the following:
re-established land, being all of the land in the CAA3 that, on the re-establishment date, has achieved re-establishment:
non-established land, being all of the land in the CAA3 that, on the re-establishment date, has not achieved re-establishment and is not permanently affected land:
if the adverse event was a natural event, permanently affected land, being all of the land in the CAA3—
that, on the re-establishment date, has not achieved re-establishment; and
on which the adverse event has permanently prevented re-establishing a forest; and
include a final forestry emissions return under section 183B for the activity—
that covers each CAA3; and
that uses the re-establishment date as the relevant date; and
include in that return a new unit balance report under section 185 that covers the following carbon accounting areas (each a CAA4) formed from each CAA3:
1 or more re-established CAA4s for any re-established land in the CAA3:
a non-established CAA4 for any non-established land in the CAA3:
a permanently affected CAA4 for any permanently affected land in the CAA3; and
include any information prescribed in regulations made under section 193R.
The notice must—
be signed by the participant; and
be given within 60 working days after the re-establishment date; and
be given—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
In relation to the final forestry emissions return and the new unit balance report required by subsection (2)﻿(b) and (c), sections 183B to 185 apply as if—
the references in those sections to CAA1 were references to CAA3; and
the references in those sections to CAA2 were references to CAA4.
Sections 183B to 185, and the provisions applied by them, apply as if the land in a non-established CAA4 or a permanently affected CAA4 were forest land.
193L Effect on re-establishment date
This section applies if a person gives the EPA a notice in accordance with section 193K, including a final forestry emissions return (for the CAA3s) and new unit balance report (for the CAA4s).
Starting on the re-establishment date,—
the emissions return for the CAA3s is treated as being submitted (so that the total liability or entitlement has effect, and the unit balance is updated, for the CAA3s under section 184D); and
the person is not liable to surrender the unit balance of each CAA3; and
for each re-established CAA4,—
the person is the participant in respect of the re-established CAA4 (instead of the CAA3); and
the land in the re-established CAA4 remains temporary adverse event land; and
the unit balance of the re-established CAA4 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report; and
for each non-established CAA4,—
the person is a participant in respect of the non-established CAA4; and
the land in the non-established CAA4 ceases to be temporary adverse event land; and
the unit balance of the non-established CAA4 is the opening unit balance calculated for it in the new unit balance report; and
section 193Q applies to the land; and
for each permanently affected CAA4,—
the person ceases to be a participant in respect of the permanently affected CAA4; and
the land in the permanently affected CAA4 ceases to be temporary adverse event land; and
the person is not liable to surrender the unit balance of each permanently affected CAA4.
The EPA must amend the register kept under section 57, and the records of carbon accounting areas kept under section 182C(2), to record the effects of this section.
 Carbon recovery
193M Carbon recovery criteria
A hectare of temporary adverse event land achieves carbon recovery when the carbon stock of the hectare (determined in accordance with regulations made under section 193R) is equal to the pre-event carbon stock rate.
193N Notice when land achieves carbon recovery
When temporary adverse event land achieves carbon recovery, the participant in respect of the land must give notice to the EPA that the land has achieved carbon recovery.
The notice must—
be signed by the participant; and
be given when the next emissions return that covers the land is submitted; and
be given—
in the prescribed manner and format; and
together with the prescribed fee (if any); and
together with the prescribed information (if any).
If the participant gives notice in relation to land under this section before giving the notice required for the land under section 193K, the land is released from being temporary adverse event land with effect from the date on which the notice under section 193K takes effect under section 193L.
If the participant has given notice in relation to land under section 193K before giving notice under this section, the land is released from being temporary adverse event land with effect from when carbon recovery was achieved.
 Ceasing to be temporary adverse event land before recovery
193O Cancellation for breach of conditions
If the EPA is satisfied that a condition applying under section 193E(2) has not been met in respect of temporary adverse event land in a carbon accounting area, the EPA may cancel the approval in respect of all of the land in the carbon accounting area.
If the approval is cancelled,—
the land ceases to be temporary adverse event land; and
section 193Q applies to the land.
Before cancelling an approval, the EPA must—
notify the participant of its intention to do so and the grounds for doing so; and
give the participant at least 60 working days to—
rectify the non-compliance; or
show cause as to why the EPA should not cancel the approval.
If the EPA cancels an approval, it must give the participant notice of—
its decision and the reasons for it; and
the date on which the cancellation occurred; and
the person’s right to seek a review of the decision under section 144.
193P Other circumstances causing land to cease to be temporary adverse event land
Temporary adverse event land is intentionally converted if the participant—
takes any action that is inconsistent with the land achieving re-establishment; or
otherwise takes any action for the purpose of converting the land to land that is not forest land.
If temporary adverse event land is intentionally converted,—
the land ceases to be temporary adverse event land; and
the land is to be treated as deforested (despite section 179A); and
the reversion date for section 193Q is the date on which the first action referred to in subsection (1)﻿(a) or (b) occurred.
If re-established land is cleared after its re-establishment date but before it achieves carbon recovery,—
the land ceases to be temporary adverse event land; and
the land is to be treated as deforested; and
the reversion date for section 193Q is the date on which the clearing commenced.
If re-established land becomes land that is to be treated as deforested under section 179(1)﻿(b) or (c) before it achieves carbon recovery,—
the land ceases to be temporary adverse event land; and
the reversion date for section 193Q is the 10-year or 20-year date under section 179.
193Q Consequences if land ceases to be temporary adverse event land
This section applies to the following land:
non-established land that ceases to be temporary adverse event land under section 193L(2)﻿(d), for which the reversion date is the re-establishment date:
land in a carbon accounting area in respect of which the approval is cancelled under section 193O, for which the reversion date is the date of the cancellation:
land that ceases to be temporary adverse event land under section 193P, for which the reversion date is the date specified in that section.
Starting on the reversion date, the provisions of this Act apply to the land as if the land had never become temporary adverse event land.
As a result, the participant must include all the emissions and removals for the land on and after the event date (including as a result of the adverse event) in the next emissions return the participant is required to submit.
For that purpose,—
all of those emissions and removals are to be treated as having occurred on the reversion date; but
the emissions resulting from the adverse event are to be determined by reference to the pre-event carbon stock rate for the land.
Section 193H(2)﻿(e) applies to a reconfiguration if—
the land to which this section applies is only part of a carbon accounting area; and
as a result of subsection (2), the participant is required to reconfigure that carbon accounting area to remove that land.
If the activity on the land is permanent forestry, see also section 190C(2).
 Regulations
193R Regulations for temporary adverse events
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
prescribing circumstances and dates for the definitions of event date and re-establishment date in sections 193 and 193J:
prescribing the methodology for determining—
the pre-event carbon stock rate (section 193):
carbon stock loss (section 193A):
carbon stock for the purpose of determining carbon recovery (section 193M):
prescribing the kinds of events that are adverse events (section 193A):
prescribing—
the minimum affected area (section 193A(1)﻿(d)):
the minimum carbon stock loss (section 193A(1)﻿(e)):
prescribing other information to be included in, and the submission date and other requirements for, applications made under section 193A:
prescribing notification requirements and other criteria for approval under section 193B:
prescribing conditions for the purposes of section 193E(2):
providing that a participant for temporary adverse event land is not required to—
calculate emissions and removals for which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units:
submit emissions returns covering a carbon accounting area in relation to which they are not liable to surrender, or entitled to receive, units (section 193F(3)):
prescribing other information to be included in, and other requirements for, notices under section 193K:
providing for any other matters contemplated by this subpart, necessary for its administration, or necessary for giving it full effect.
Regulations under this section may make different provision for different cases on any differential basis.
Regulations made under this section may require the use of a computer programme available via the Internet site of the EPA.
Regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(g) may relate to emissions or removals that—
stem directly from an activity; or
are associated with a product or other thing that is the subject of an activity.
See sections 3A and 3B for consultation requirements that apply to the making of regulations under this section.
Regulations made under this section come into force 3 months after the date of their publication under the Legislation Act 2019, or on any later date specified in the regulations.
See also sections 169 to 175 (incorporation by reference).
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
 Subpart 8 — General
 Input returns may be submitted before actual emissions returns
194 Input returns may be submitted for certain emissions returns for forestry activities
This section applies before a person submits an emissions return (for a forestry activity) of a type specified in the regulations.
The person may first submit, for the activity and 1 or more of the areas or carbon accounting areas covered by the emissions return, an input return that contains the data or information required by the regulations.
The input return must be submitted by—
the deadline specified in the regulations; or
any extended deadline granted by the EPA under the regulations.
In this section, regulations means regulations made under section 194B.
194A EPA may do calculations based on input return
This section applies if the EPA receives an input return in accordance with section 194.
As soon as practicable after receipt, the EPA must—
calculate for each area or carbon accounting area covered by the input return, as required for the relevant emissions return,—
the participant’s emissions and removals; and
the participant’s liability to surrender units for their emissions or entitlement to receive New Zealand units for their removals; and
give a notice to the participant that includes—
the calculations and the calculated amounts; and
the data, information, or other matters on which the calculations are based; and
a statement that the participant may choose to include the calculations and the calculated amounts in the relevant emissions return; and
a statement about the effect of subsection (3).
The EPA is not liable for anything that results from the calculations that it makes in good faith under this section, and the EPA’s calculations and notice do not affect any obligation of the participant under this Act (such as the obligation to submit an accurate emissions return).
194B Regulations for input returns
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
specifying the 1 or more types of emissions return for a forestry activity for which input returns may be submitted, which may be specified by reference to 1 or more of the following:
the type of forestry activity:
any feature of the forest or land to which the activity relates:
any other matter:
specifying the data or information that must be contained in any input return or the input return for each type of emissions return:
specifying the deadline for submitting the input return for each type of emissions return, which must be a reasonable period before the deadline for submitting the emissions return:
providing for how, and for how long, the EPA may extend a deadline for submitting the input return for any emissions return or for each type of emissions return:
authorising the EPA to issue guidelines or standards in relation to the matters specified under paragraphs (b) to (d).
See sections 3A and 3B for consultation requirements that apply to the making of the regulations.
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
If regulations authorise the EPA to issue guidelines or standards under subsection (1)﻿(e),—
those guidelines or standards are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements); and
the regulations must contain a statement to that effect.
A person who has complied with guidelines or standards issued by the EPA under regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(e) is, in the absence of proof to the contrary, presumed to have complied with the relevant requirements specified in regulations corresponding to those guidelines or standards.
194C Regulations for input returns
 Notification of status of forest land
195 Notification of status of forest land
The EPA must, if required by regulations made under section 168, notify the following persons of the details of the land that the EPA is satisfied is a type of land described in subsection (2):
the Registrar of the Maori Land Court in whose jurisdiction the land is situated in relation to Maori land; and
the Registrar-General of Land in relation to land that is registered under the Land Transfer Act 2017; and
the Registrar of Deeds in relation to land that is registered under the Deeds Registration Act 1908.
The types of land are—
the following types of land in respect of which a person is registered as a participant:
pre-1990 forest land:
P90 offsetting land:
post-1989 forest land:
the following types of post-1989 forest land:
P89 offsetting (approved) land (as defined in section 192):
temporary adverse event land:
land for which a person is registered as a participant in permanent forestry:
land that the EPA has declared to be exempt land.
On receipt of a notice under subsection (1), the Registrar-General of Land or the Registrar of the Maori Land Court or the Registrar of Deeds must record the notice on the appropriate register under the Land Transfer Act 2017, record of the Maori Land Court, or deeds index under the Deeds Registration Act 1908.
The Registrar-General of Land or the Registrar of the Maori Land Court or the Registrar of Deeds must cancel any notices recorded under subsection (3) if required under regulations made under section 168.
 Information about status of forest land
195A Information about status of forest land
Despite anything in this Act, the EPA must, on receipt of a written request for information about the carbon accounting area or areas to which it relates, provide a statement containing the information in subsection (2) to—
the landowner of any post-1989 forest land in respect of which the holder of a registered forestry right or registered lease or party to a Crown conservation contract is a participant; or
a prospective transferee, holder of a registered forestry right or registered lease, or party to a Crown conservation contract who has the written consent of the participant in respect of any post-1989 forest land.
A statement under subsection (1) must set out—
the emissions returns (if any) that have been submitted in respect of the carbon accounting area or areas covered by the information request since the carbon accounting area or areas were constituted, and the period covered by those returns; and
the unit balance of the carbon accounting area or areas covered by the information request.
 Forestry classifications of land
196 Meaning of forestry classification
In this Act, forestry classification means 1 or more classifications of an area of land that—
classifies the area by whether or how—
a definition or matter in this Act that relates to forestry applies to the area; or
the area is eligible to have a definition or matter in this Act apply to it if certain requirements are satisfied; and
is given—
by the EPA under section 196A (initial classification), 196C (change of classification to correct error), 196D (change of classification to update for changes), or 144 (review of classification); or
by the decision of the District Court or High Court under section 145 or 146.
Examples
If specified by regulations, an area of land might be classified as—
pre-1990 forest land:
post-1989 forest land:
land that is eligible to become post-1989 forest land (if it becomes forest land):
P90 offsetting land:
land that has been deforested, or deforested on specified dates:
land that is eligible to be declared exempt land under section 180E (because of tree weeds):
post-1989 forest land for which a participant is registered for standard forestry that is or is not a carbon accounting area (averaging):
post-1989 forest land for which a participant is registered for permanent forestry:
land that was forest land on 31 December 1989:
exempt land:
pre-1990 forest land to which the pre-1990 forest land allocation plan applies:
something else.
196A EPA may give forestry classifications to areas of land
The EPA may give 1 or more forestry classifications to an area of land in accordance with regulations made under section 196F.
196A Power to withdraw or suspend certain draft allocation plans
196B Effect of forestry classifications
The forestry classification of an area of land is conclusive evidence of how the relevant definition or matter in this Act applies to the area.
The EPA must apply this Act to the area in accordance with the forestry classification.
If a person’s application, notice, emissions return, or other document under this Act specifies the forestry classification of an area of land, the document—
need not include any information that is covered by the forestry classification; but
for a forestry classification that an area of land is eligible for something if certain requirements are satisfied, must include information about whether the requirements are satisfied.
The EPA, or any person carrying out its powers, duties, or functions,—
does not warrant that any forestry classification is correct and not based on, or affected by, something that is incorrect or that has materially changed; and
is not liable for anything that results from a forestry classification being incorrect or based on, or affected by, something that is incorrect or that has materially changed, as long as the forestry classification was given in good faith.
196C Change of forestry classification to correct error
The EPA may change the forestry classification of an area of land to correct any error that the EPA is satisfied is contained in the classification, including where the classification was based on incorrect information.
The EPA must make the change in accordance with regulations made under section 196F.
196D Change of forestry classification to update for changes
The EPA may change the forestry classification of an area of land if—
there is a material change in any of the information or facts on which the classification is based; or
there is a material change to this Act, or to any regulations made under this Act, that affects the classification.
The EPA must make the change in accordance with regulations made under section 196F.
196E Forestry classification with effect before date classification given
This section applies if a forestry classification has effect before the date on which the classification is given, whether—
by the EPA under section 196A, 196C, or 196D or on review under section 144; or
by the decision of a court on appeal under section 145 or 146.
The forestry classification must be ignored in respect of the period before the date on which the classification is given—
to the extent that it would increase the number of units that a person is required to surrender, or decrease the number of New Zealand units that a person is entitled to receive, in respect of that period; and
in respect of any other matter specified by regulations made under section 196F.
In all other respects, the forestry classification must be applied to that period.
To avoid doubt, where the forestry classification is ignored under subsection (2), the earlier forestry classification (if any) applies instead.
196F Regulations for forestry classifications
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
specifying the forestry classifications that the EPA may give to areas of land:
prescribing 1 or more methods or processes by which the EPA may give a new or changed forestry classification to an area, and those methods or processes—
may or may not provide for a person to apply for a classification; and
may prescribe the fees or charges payable by an applicant for a classification to enable the recovery of all or part of the direct and indirect costs of the EPA in—
receiving and processing the application; and
considering, granting, or declining the application; and
must require the EPA to first consult the persons that the regulations specify are likely to be substantially affected by the classification, unless the only persons likely to be substantially affected have applied for, or agreed to, the classification:
providing for when a forestry classification comes into effect, which may, for example,—
subject to section 196E, be before the date on which the classification is given if the classification is changed under section 196C or 196D, on review by the EPA under section 144, or on appeal to the court under section 145 or 146:
differ for different forestry classifications or circumstances, such as whether a person is responsible for a material change described in section 196D(1)﻿(a):
specifying matters for the purposes of section 196E(2)﻿(b) (in respect of which a forestry classification is ignored for the period before the date on which it is given):
providing for the publication of the following in 1 or more notices, instruments, maps, or tools, which may be electronic:
any decision to give a forestry classification to an area of land:
the current forestry classifications of all areas of land, and any related matters.
Examples of the costs that may be recovered under regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(ii) include (but are not limited to)—
the costs of providing, operating, and maintaining systems, databases, and other processes in connection with the application:
the costs of services provided by third parties.
Section 167(4) also applies to regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(b)﻿(ii).
See sections 3A and 3B for consultation requirements that apply to the making of regulations under this section.
Regulations made under this section come into force 3 months after the date of their publication under the Legislation Act 2019, or on any later date specified in the regulations.
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
 Grant-funded forests
197 Entitlement to units for removals from grant-funded forests
A participant in an activity of standard forestry or permanent forestry in a carbon accounting area is not entitled to receive New Zealand units for removals that—
are attributable to forest species in relation to which the participant has received a grant from the Crown under a grant scheme relating to forestry that is prescribed in regulations made under section 197A (a grant-funded forest); and
occur during the stand-down period for that forest prescribed in regulations made under section 197A.
197A Regulations for grant-funded forests
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council made on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations for 1 or more of the following purposes:
prescribing Crown grant schemes relating to forestry:
prescribing stand-down periods for grant-funded forests:
prescribing methodologies for attributing removals to grant-funded forests:
providing for any other matters contemplated by section 197, necessary for its administration, or necessary for giving it full effect.
Regulations made under this section may make different provision for different cases on any differential basis, including—
for different grant schemes:
for different periods of time:
for different forest species:
for different parts of New Zealand.
Regulations made under this section may require the use of a computer programme available via the Internet site of the EPA.
Regulations made under subsection (1)﻿(c) may relate to emissions or removals that—
stem directly from the relevant activity; or
are associated with a product or other thing that is the subject of the relevant activity.
Regulations under this section are secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements).
 Part 5A — Sector-specific provisions: liquid fossil fuels
 Subpart 2 — Liquid fossil fuels sector
198 Registration as participant by purchasers of obligation fuel
An application under section 57 to be registered as a participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4 may be submitted to the EPA at any time.
If the EPA registers a person as a participant under section 57 in respect of an activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4, the registration takes effect 12 months after the date of the notice issued under section 57(6).
If the EPA has received an application under section 58 for removal of a person’s name from the register as a participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4, the EPA must remove, under section 58(4), the applicant’s name from the register on the date that is 48 months after the notice issued under section 58(3)﻿(b).
The participant who is registered, or removed from registration, under this section must give notice of that matter, and the date that it took or takes effect, to every person who is registered under section 56 in respect of an activity in Part 2 of Schedule 3.
The notice must be given in writing or electronically as soon as practicable after the participant receives the EPA’s notice about, or becomes aware of, the matter.
The EPA must provide the participant with any address that it has recorded for each person who must be notified.
199 Historical information sufficient to satisfy EPA
A person who carries out an activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4 may, in an application to register as a participant in respect of that activity submitted under section 57, include with the application information about the total volume of obligation fuel purchased by the person in the year prior to the year in which the person submits the application (and any other prior years the person wishes).
If the EPA receives an application under section 57 that includes the information specified in subsection (1), the EPA may, for the purposes of section 57(4), satisfy itself that the person is, or will be when the person’s registration takes effect, carrying out the activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4 on the basis of that information.
Nothing in this section prevents the EPA from requiring a person specified in subsection (1) to provide any further information that the EPA requires to satisfy him or herself that the person is, (or will, when the person's registration takes effect, be) carrying out the activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4.
200 Effect of purchasing less than threshold level of obligation fuel
If a person is a participant in respect of the activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4, and in any year the volume of obligation fuel that the person purchases is less than, or the person knows that the volume purchased will be less than, the threshold specified in Part 3 of Schedule 4—
the person is not required to notify the EPA under section 59(1) that the person has ceased, or will cease, to carry out the activity; and
the EPA must not, under section 59(2), treat the person as having ceased to carry out the activity; and
the person remains a participant in respect of the activity until the person’s name is removed, in accordance with this Act, from the register that is kept for the purposes of section 57.
201 Effect of registration by purchasers of obligation fuel
A participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 2 of Schedule 3 is not required to surrender units in respect of obligation fuel that is purchased by a person who is a participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 3 of Schedule 4.
202 Activities added to Part 2 of Schedule 3
The Governor-General may, by Order in Council, made on the recommendation of the Minister, amend Part 2 of Schedule 3 by adding activities relating to the following matters:
owning or operating a ship onto which goods are loaded at any port in New Zealand for carriage to and unloading at any other port in New Zealand, if the ship consumes fuel that is purchased—
outside New Zealand; or
in New Zealand, but in respect of which no participant is required to surrender units under this Act; or
fishing within New Zealand’s exclusive economic zone, if the vessel used for fishing consumes fuel that is purchased outside New Zealand.
The Minister may only recommend the making of an Order in Council under subsection (1) if—
no participant is, prior to making the recommendation, liable to surrender units in respect of the emissions from the fuel consumed while the activity that is to be the subject of the recommendation is carried out; and
adding the activity is—
necessary to ensure that A is similar to B, where—
A is the cost increases that a person carrying out the activity will face, if the activity is added, due to the obligation imposed by this Act on the person to surrender units in respect of the emissions from the fuel consumed while carrying out the activity; and
B is the cost increases that a person carrying out a comparable activity faces due to any obligations imposed by this Act on persons carrying out an activity listed in Part 2 of Schedule 3 to surrender units in respect of the emissions from the fuel consumed while the comparable activity is carried out; and
not inconsistent with international climate change obligations; and
recommending the order will not result in costs to the Crown that exceed the benefits that the Crown expects to receive after the order is made.
An Order in Council made under subsection (1) takes effect for the removal activity or activities concerned on and from—
1 January of the next year, if made on or before 30 June in any year; or
1 July of the next year, if made on or after 1 July in any year.
An order under this section—
is secondary legislation (see Part 3 of the Legislation Act 2019 for publication requirements); and
must be confirmed by an Act (see subpart 3 of Part 5 of the Legislation Act 2019).
202A Orders are confirmable instruments
203 Treatment of obligation fuels
This section applies if, in breach of the Customs and Excise Act 2018, a participant fails to remove obligation fuel for home consumption.
If this section applies, the obligation fuel that was not removed for home consumption must, for the purposes of this Act, be treated as obligation fuel removed for home consumption under the Customs and Excise Act 2018.
 Part 5B — Sector-specific provisions: stationary energy
 Subpart 3 — Stationary energy sector
204 Participant with respect to mining coal or natural gas
This section applies if the following activities listed in Part 3 of Schedule 3 are carried out—
mining coal where the volume of coal mined exceeds 2 000 tonnes in a year:
mining natural gas, other than for export.
If this section applies and—
a permit is required under the Crown Minerals Act 1991 to carry out the mining, then the person who holds the permit is to be treated as the person carrying out the activity; or
no permit is required to carry out the mining, then the owner of the mine is to be treated as the person carrying out the activity.
Despite subsection (2)﻿(a), subsection (4) applies if—
a permit relating to mining coal is held by 2 or more persons jointly under terms that entitle the individual holders to a proportion of the coal mined under the permit; or
a permit relating to mining natural gas is held by 2 or more persons jointly under terms that entitle the individual holders to a proportion of the gas mined under the permit.
If this subsection applies,—
section 157 does not apply; and
each of the individual holders referred to in subsection (3)—
is to be treated as the person carrying out the activity referred to in subsection (1) in relation to any natural gas or coal (as applicable) to which the person is entitled under the permit; and
must comply with the obligations of a participant under this Act in relation to the natural gas or coal (as applicable) to which the person is entitled under the permit.
205 Mining natural gas in exclusive economic zone and continental shelf
This Act applies to the activity of mining natural gas, other than for export, listed in Part 3 of Schedule 3, if that activity is carried out anywhere within the territorial limits of New Zealand, the exclusive economic zone, or in, on, or above the continental shelf.
To avoid doubt, a person who carries out the activity of mining natural gas, other than for export, anywhere within the territorial limits of New Zealand, the exclusive economic zone, or in, on, or above the continental shelf is not to be treated as importing the natural gas mined from that activity for the purposes of this Act.
For the purposes of this section,—
continental shelf has the same meaning as in section 2(1) of the Continental Shelf Act 1964
exclusive economic zone has the same meaning as in section 9 of the Territorial Sea, Contiguous Zone, and Exclusive Economic Zone Act 1977.
206 Obligation with respect to combusting used oil, waste oil, and waste
A participant who carries out the activity of combusting used oil, waste oil, used tyres, or waste for the purpose of generating electricity or industrial heat listed in Part 3 of Schedule 3 is not required to surrender units in respect of any—
emissions that result from the combustion of used oil or waste oil if the used oil or waste oil combusted is an obligation fuel; or
carbon dioxide that results from the combustion of waste that is organic waste.
207 Obligation with respect to mining coal
A participant who carries out the activity of mining coal, where the volume of coal mined exceeds 2 000 tonnes in a year, listed in Part 3 of Schedule 3—
is not required to surrender units in respect of any carbon dioxide emissions from any coal that is exported:
is required to surrender units in respect of any coal seam gas emissions that result from the activity.
208 Purchase of coal or natural gas from certain related companies of Part 3 of Schedule 3 participant
For the purposes of the activities listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4, the reference to a participant who mines coal or natural gas includes the following persons:
a wholly owned subsidiary of a participant who mines coal or natural gas:
a holding company of which a participant who mines coal or natural gas is a wholly owned subsidiary:
another wholly owned subsidiary of a holding company of which a participant who mines coal or natural gas is the wholly owned subsidiary.
In subsection (1), subsidiary and holding company have the same meaning as in section 5 of the Companies Act 1993.
209 Registration as participant by purchasers of coal or natural gas
An application under section 57 to be registered as a participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4 may be submitted to the EPA at any time.
If the EPA registers a person as a participant under section 57 in respect of an activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4, the registration takes effect 12 months from the date of the notice issued under section 57(6).
If the EPA has received an application under section 58 for removal of a person’s name from the register as a participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4, the EPA must remove, under section 58(4), the applicant’s name from the register on the date that is 48 months after the date of the notice issued under section 58(3)﻿(b).
The participant who is registered, or removed from registration, under this section in respect of an activity must give notice of that matter, and the date that it took or takes effect, to every person who—
mines—
coal, if the activity is purchasing coal; or
natural gas, if the activity is purchasing natural gas; and
is registered under section 56.
The notice must be given in writing or electronically as soon as practicable after the participant receives the EPA’s notice about, or becomes aware of, the matter.
The EPA must provide the participant with any address that it has recorded for each person who must be notified.
210 Historical information sufficient to satisfy EPA
A person who carries out an activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4 may, in an application to register as a participant in respect of that activity submitted under section 57, include with the application information about the total volume of coal or natural gas, as the case may be, purchased by the person in the year prior to the year in which the person submits the application (and any other prior years the person wishes).
If the EPA receives an application under section 57 that includes the information specified in subsection (1), the EPA may, for the purposes of section 57(4), satisfy itself that the person is (or will, when the person’s registration takes effect, be) carrying out an activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4 that is specified in the application on the basis of that information.
Nothing in this section prevents the EPA from requiring a person specified in subsection (1) to provide any further information that the EPA requires to satisfy itself that the person is, or will be when the person’s registration takes effect, carrying out the activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4 that is specified in the application.
211 Effect of purchasing less than threshold level of coal or natural gas
If a person is a participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4, and in any year the volume of coal or natural gas that the person purchases is less than, or the person knows that the volume purchased will be less than, the thresholds specified in Part 4 of Schedule 4,—
the person is not required to notify the EPA under section 59(1) that the person has ceased, or will cease, to carry out the activity; and
the EPA must not, under section 59(2), treat the person as having ceased to carry out the activity; and
the person remains a participant in respect of the activity until the person’s name is removed, in accordance with this Act, from the register that is kept for the purposes of section 57.
212 Effect of registration by purchasers of coal or natural gas
A participant who mines coal or mines natural gas is not required to surrender units in respect of coal or natural gas that is purchased by a person who is a participant in respect of an activity listed in Part 4 of Schedule 4.
